Recorded Tool readers now share the literal code reader's local Select All boundary. Whole-reader copy uses the current presented rows or preview excerpt; expanded command output supplies the complete ANSI-stripped captured source, including virtualized lines and trailing newlines. The generic Tool inspector also preserves the original JSON tokens in its result and secondary Arguments. Two-space indentation edits source whitespace instead of recreating values: large IDs, precise decimals, exponent spellings, negative zero, duplicate members and string escapes survive both reading and copying. Invalid structured text remains literal. The existing result-first hierarchy, CodeBody selection/copy and direct raw-argument action retain their owners.

MCP configuration editors now retire obsolete connection-test feedback when a field changes. Cancel retires the editor while the existing command owner finishes admitted work; an old completion cannot close a newly opened draft. Failed commands retain the exact editable draft and the user's current focus. JSON import now rejects source names that collapse to one canonical name before admitting any create. The parser owns collision data; the view localizes recovery copy and renders each original/final name as literal text. Successful order and rename notices retain their existing owners.

Provider Save & Test now admits its feedback lease before saving. An edit during the save retires both later test outcomes and that compound operation's save failure. The accepted update still commits through the existing mutation owner, and later URL/key drafts remain editable and require a new explicit submission. Current success and failures remain visible. Ordinary Save retains its established Saved acknowledgement while preserving subsequent edits; that separate contract was not redesigned.

The original working tree is clean at this checkpoint. These features are implemented; do not recreate them from the older handoff inventory.

The user has approved the brand direction. Its production assets, settings pane, translations and source artwork are committed:

- `/Users/tangerg/Desktop/flame/brand` contains the primary mark, dimensional companion, plush character and usage guidance.
- `/Users/tangerg/Desktop/flame/design/branding` contains concept and refinement assets.

Composer submission captures the exact immutable draft and Session at the authoring owner. Slash handlers explicitly accept input or admit a send; returning from a handler alone does not clear the draft. Accepted input records history in its captured Session, consumes only the submitted revision, preserves later text and attachment edits, and avoids stealing another control's focus. Replacing Runtime storage retires old callbacks. Goal reports acceptance through this same owner rather than comparing text or clearing independently.

The pasted-text repair keeps those submission and storage contracts. The
required `restorePaste(id, text)` intent accepts the current reviewed text in one
draft transition, removes only that paste and preserves the typed prefix and
other attachments. Empty reviewed text removes the paste without resurrecting
its old body; a missing ID remains a no-op. `editPaste` reports whether the owner
kept the exact paste, so the view does not repeat the domain's blank-text rule.
Cancel/Escape and ordinary Save return to the surviving chip; Put back and a Save
that removes it return to the existing Composer textarea. One local close intent
captures the focus origin and target before a retiring action. The resolver
suppresses a return on unrelated later focus, retirement without a close intent
or a disconnected target. `TextEditorDialog` only forwards the shared modal's
existing `finalFocus` option. The native scenarios and qualification boundaries
are recorded below.

The implementation lives under `/Users/tangerg/Desktop/flame/apps/web/src/features/chat/composer` and `/Users/tangerg/Desktop/flame/apps/web/src/features/chat/goal`, with updated shell and SDK consumers. Focus is owned by the installed Composer state port; the former `application/focus.ts` was intentionally removed. Do not restore the retired clear, text-comparison or global-focus paths.

CLI slash commands preserve the editor, attachments and cursor when command lookup, availability or arguments are rejected. Contributed commands accept only after their cancellable Session operation starts. Approval normalization retains an allow or deny decision when remembered scope becomes unavailable; unknown choices cannot create an answerable form.

Start each new task with the actual status and relevant diffs. Preserve any newer unrelated edits, avoid bulk formatting or staging, and do not treat the old unfinished-file inventory as current work.

## Goal rail and Plan reading

The user's supplied Codex screenshot supersedes the earlier Goal rail hierarchy.
`features/goal/ui/GoalStatusSurface.tsx` now gives status the existing semibold
foreground role and the objective a secondary, single-line truncated role. The
rail is inset by `space.s3` on both sides and overlaps behind the Composer by the
same amount. It retains the shared Composer corner, glass and edge roles. The
trailing group contains creation age, Clear, circular Pause/Resume and the
existing objective editor with an expand glyph. Both the summary and that last
action open the same editor; cancellation returns to the actual opener. Narrow
space permits a separate action row instead of clipping or shrinking controls.

Age is derived only from Runtime's existing `createdAt`, including time paused.
Its localized title and accessible text explicitly say time since Goal creation;
it is not execution time or a new lifecycle fact. The existing duration formatter
keeps the product's hour/minute precision. Invalid timestamps omit this reading,
and a future timestamp clamps to zero. The technical interval hook moved from
the shell into `lib/useElapsedMillis.ts` for the two actual consumers, with no
duplicate timer implementation. It neither writes commands nor rerenders the
objective draft on each tick. Existing status admission, pending/offline states,
stop reasons, errors, IME protection and generation retirement retain their owners.

`features/chat/plan-progress/ui/ActivePlan.tsx` keeps its compact standing pill
and the authoritative Session Plan identity, steps, current step and progress.
Click, Enter or Space opens the existing nonmodal Base UI Popover with a named,
focusable `ScrollRegion`. Page Down/End can read the entire list; Escape returns
to the pill and Tab proceeds to the Goal summary. Accepted Plan revisions keep
the same reader, focus and scroll position. Session/generation replacement,
absence, unmount and the existing hidden-content signal retire its local open
state. Returning to a hidden conversation requires an explicit reopen. Finished
Plans remain reviewable. No Plan command, duplicate progress rule or new page
was introduced.

The Goal screenshot was treated as an approximately 2x capture: its proportions
map to existing 12 px inset/overlap, UI-small text, semibold title and shared
compact controls rather than new tokens. ZCode's actual `TodoPanel.tsx` and
HeroUI's Popover demo provide explicit disclosure/read-task evidence; Flame does
not copy ZCode's removal of completed Plans. The [Radix Popover model](https://www.radix-ui.com/primitives/docs/components/popover)
supports a bounded, focus-managed contextual reader, while the [Tooltip model](https://www.radix-ui.com/primitives/docs/components/tooltip)
does not supply this sustained keyboard reading path. The local ChatGPT Plan
module inspected here only reexports its panel entry, so it is not used as focus
evidence. The actual Wails observations below establish that behavior.

Continue with another observed conversation → material → return path on current
main; do not turn this completed rail/read task into an unrelated redesign.

## Implemented foundation to retain

The built-in Light and Dark schemes keep achromatic canvas, drawer, surface,
elevated, sunken, neutral text and boundary roles. Accent changes cannot advance
those values. `features/theme/kit/scale.ts` now mixes only the theme's declared
canvas/ink and the existing scheme/contrast steps; there is no tint parameter or
second neutral writer. The approved Flame Orange and accent/status roles retain
their meanings, while custom/package palettes keep their own canvas and ink.
`globals.css`, the HTML bootstrap and the native opening window mirror the same
neutral canvases. A remembered built-in background from the tinting release
cannot repaint the first frame; custom/package paint projections remain valid.

The reported recurrence was real: `75fd5dd80` explicitly removed automatic tint
at the user's request, but `d345239c4` later restored it in `neutralScale` and
asserted that behavior in a test. Later main integrations retained it. The
regression is repaired at the scale owner rather than hidden by a page override.

| Product area                  | Current behavior and ownership                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| ----------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Shared presentation           | Panels, persistent controls, resource groups, disclosures, forms, modal geometry, metric summaries and semantic badges share presentation owners. The host owns appearance values; `/Users/tangerg/Desktop/flame/ui` owns reusable presentation rules. Pages compose those rules.                                                                              |
| Color and brand               | The default accent is Flame Orange. Built-in page planes, neutral ink and boundaries stay achromatic across accent changes. Content emphasis keeps its semantic accent roles; custom themes retain their own colors and the approved brand assets remain.                                                                                                      |
| Prose and layout              | Prose takes one reading step above interface text: 16 px with the default 14 px interface, while code keeps its separate preference. Paragraphs use one gap; headings have unitless leading and a visible following gap, including deeper levels. The `65ch` cap and wider code/table/media measures remain intentional.                                       |
| Markdown tables               | Long tables stay within a bounded reading viewport. Compact table actions follow the table edge and wrap when necessary. Expanded previews keep controls and column headings reachable. Do not reintroduce page overflow or remove access to long data.                                                                                                        |
| Code and recorded output      | One stable bounded code-reading region retains literal selection, scroll and focus as syntax highlighting arrives. Shared captions, wrapping and copy behavior are established. Loading messages no longer expose raw Markdown source as a temporary presentation.                                                                                             |
| Selection and file references | Ordinary action-button drags cannot select the surrounding application. Reading text and inline file references remain selectable. Dragging a file reference preserves selection without navigating the Dock; ordinary clicks and Enter/Space still open its path and line. Native copy and whitespace dismissal work.                                         |
| Tool rows                     | Rows reserve action space only when real actions exist. Recorded paths wrap at directory boundaries and retain literal copy. Expanded groups remain visible and clickable as the Dock narrows; the WebKit grid sizing repair preserves reversible transitions.                                                                                                 |
| MCP configuration drafts      | The mounted editor owns feedback and view completion. Existing MCP commands retain their captured inputs, Runtime generation and cache-repair owner. Pending field/control presentation comes from the shared primitives; import identity validation completes before command admission.                                                                       |
| Context Dock                  | File identity and return navigation precede a separate wrapping mode/action row. Full directories remain available beneath the filename. Opening from the tree focuses the return action; returning restores the tree cursor without stealing another region's focus. Panels grow through their complete content; fixed pane seams paint above child surfaces. |
| Subagent stop controls        | Shared filled stop marks and quiet circular controls replace the checkbox-like outline squares. Their placement is consistent across the narrative, Dock list and detail view; terminal entries reserve no dead cancel slot.                                                                                                                                   |
| Main and delegated messages   | Both use `MessageTurn` and `MessageBlock` for spacing, prose, bubbles, long-input folding, tools, code, tables, media and message actions. Child rows retain their own Run and referenced facts. Their overview keeps its scroll position; returning restores the originating task's focus.                                                                    |
| Motion and focus              | Shared moving item backplates remain interruptible and reversible and consume the host motion tokens. Reduced motion, scoped code Select All, native dismissal and focus return have established owners and regression coverage.                                                                                                                               |
| Authoring and acceptance      | One captured submission owns draft consumption and history. Rejected input stays editable; delayed acceptance preserves successor revisions, Session changes, attachment staging and other controls' focus. Goal and slash extensions share this settlement contract.                                                                                          |

File-reference gesture behavior lives in `/Users/tangerg/Desktop/flame/apps/web/src/features/chat/file-references/public/FileRefLink.tsx`: pointer activation consults the browser's existing selection; keyboard activation remains available. It introduces no separate selection state. Read current callers and coverage before changing this boundary.

`FileReferencePresentationContext` carries only a transient presentation's source ref and completion callback. `MarkdownTable` supplies it to the preview copy, leaving inline references unchanged. The existing file command still owns navigation and the existing inspection owner still records ancestry, previous inspection and active focus. One local `closed`/`open`/`navigated` state selects the preview's close policy. `LightboxDialog` forwards Base UI's typed `finalFocus` option; a file navigation suppresses dialog refocus while ordinary dismissal keeps the default. No route observer, focus retry, new navigation record or Runtime contract was added.

Activity disclosures now retain their quiet chevron before hover, making the
summary-to-evidence route discoverable. Their existing toggle, rotation and
reduced-motion owners remain in use.

The native scrolling background defect came from shared `Panel` roots shrinking
inside a column flex viewport: long children overflowed below the panel's canvas
and sticky-control boundary. `Panel` now opts out of that shrinking. The weak Dock
separation had a different cause: the inset seam painted beneath opaque child
reading surfaces. The conversation plane's fixed edge now paints the same
full-plane inset wash as the left sidebar edge, without a persistent
dark stroke or additional hover/drag darkening. Both edges share one wash rule;
the resize cursor and keyboard focus treatment remain available. Do not cover
either defect with per-view background patches or duplicate dividers.

The selected-subagent view no longer assembles a parallel message UI. Agent's
existing fact slicer projects each child message for the common renderer, so
running siblings do not suppress a finished child's actions. The same streaming
preference applies in both narratives. Copy and feedback retain the selected
message identity; regeneration appears only for a main-conversation message that
the existing replay operation can locate. Main and child scroll following remain
independent, while sharing the reading-focus guard and latest-content control.
Jumping to the bottom transfers focus to that reader before hiding the control.
The running-status pill keeps one line and grows with text size instead of
wrapping outside a fixed-height background under narrow-window pressure.

Tool groups and narrative waves now use the same `ToolActivitySummary` in the
shared message renderer. Their compact secondary line retains ongoing calls,
settled problems and refusals when folded beside the later answer. A nonzero
command exit and an error on the same call count once; provisional exit values
on running calls remain in the running
count. Refusals remain distinct from execution failures. Individual failure
marks use the Runtime status independently of optional explanatory text. The
existing automatic expansion and user-pinning rules remain in force.

The portable tool-result renderer now keeps one text control for output expansion
and collapse. Explicit expansion enters its bounded reader after positioning,
while collapse keeps the original control focused; a later user focus choice
still takes precedence. Long recorded results show their current row count in the
shared caption. Continuing a page reveals its first new row and focuses the
existing named result viewport, including the final page where the continuation
button disappears. Main and delegated messages consume this same renderer.
Sticky activity summaries now publish their measured height to the reading body,
using the established disclosure-clearance property. Keyboard targets reserve
that space when revealed, so output controls remain below a wrapped summary at
large text sizes. The shared activity component owns measurement and retires its
observer and scoped value with the component.

File references, tool material actions and the header worktree summary now
capture their source as an explicit inspection intent in Workspace navigation.
The Dock receives focus without stealing a later user choice, and its shared
return action or unhandled Escape restores the originating view and anchor.
Returning from a file opened inside a child narrative selects that same
subagent pane and retains its reader state. The source anchor can be revealed
after a width change. Background file updates keep the established outside-focus
guard; file-to-tree navigation remains independent. The inspection record is
ephemeral, retired on Session changes or navigation-port reinstallation and excluded from storage.
An unchanged child reader no longer replays its initial Back-button focus when
React Activity resumes it; native acceptance exposed this ownership conflict.

Task links in the main and child narratives now enter that same inspection owner.
The currently selected task uses the existing selected surface, a persistent open
mark and `aria-current`; its name remains owned by the enclosing delegation group
rather than repeated on every sibling. A task opened from another task retains
that source reader through React Activity. Only readers on the active inspection
path remain mounted, and leaving the path releases them. Each explicit step keeps
its source selection and preceding inspection. Returning restores the source task
and control; returning to a file also restores the file path and line captured
before another file replaced it. A new source in the conversation starts a new
path. The path stays ephemeral and retires with its Session.

The Dock's existing Back action and unhandled Escape return one inspection step.
Returning to the conversation focuses its original task summary while leaving the
inspected material available for comparison. The task header's separate **All
subagents** command opens the retained index and ends the nested task trail; it
uses a list mark rather than implying another Back action. Overview-to-task entry
keeps its existing focus and scroll behavior. The selected-reader focus effect
recognizes an inspection return and leaves focus settlement to the shared owner;
otherwise it could override the restored source with the index command.

`ChatPanel` derives `fullPaneViewId` from the explicit main route or a matching active inspection when `canPresentDock` is false. An unmeasured row, merely remembered tabs and background updates do not establish a new full-pane destination. Existing ResizeObserver, width ratio, retained-view and content-visibility owners remain in use. The catalog renders its same component in either presentation, rather than becoming an unavailable workspace renderer.

`WorkspaceWindowHeader` receives one close-or-return exit. A matching full-pane inspection uses the same translated source label and handler as the Dock; unrelated main views keep Close. Open in context dock is available only when it fits. `useInspectionFocus` consumes the actual presented id as well as the explicit route: entry and return settle in a visible region, and a Dock container losing its focus role hands its own focus to the full pane. Later controls, local Escape and IME retain priority. No retry, frame loop or duplicate focus state was introduced.

Ordinary full-pane entry also transfers focus that still belongs to the Conversation or Dock content hidden by that presentation. The same host fact covers retained view wrappers and the Activity-hidden panel catalog without inspecting their different hiding mechanisms. Focus already inside the visible destination, outside this workspace, or in a child that remains visible while moving back into the Dock is preserved. Unchanged presentations do not replay entry. Existing inspection returns and ordinary Close keep their distinct navigation policies. Explicit global Composer focus has its own presentation intent, described below.

Workspace navigation remains the sole inspection-chain owner. `inspectFrom` recognizes the explicit main source or active Dock source before retaining the previous record. The existing return command accepts an optional `dock`/`main` source presentation intent; only an explicit return to a still-open ordinary Dock source can advance that route. A previous inspection projects itself without that route change. Full-pane Files selection without an origin only changes its existing viewer. No Runtime, SDK, persisted-data or protocol migration is required.

Diff now distinguishes the general workspace entry from a file-specific entry.
The former opens a searchable changed-file overview with text-change totals; the
latter opens its requested file. Rows navigate to one selected reader rather than
expanding every file into the report. Comparison scope precedes local filtering;
file identity, layout and previous/next controls remain available while reading.
Complete directories and rename provenance stay outside the sticky identity zone.
Binary, metadata-only, partial and absent-file states keep their canonical meaning.

Local Diff history records whether the reader came from its list or directly from
a narrative source. Escape from the list-owned reader returns to the retained
query, scroll and current row; Escape from a direct file entry remains with the
host's source-return owner. The independent Files command always opens the list.
A popup dismisses before either path unwinds. Refresh retains a still-present file;
an absent target returns to the list with a notice. Failed scope reads retain the
accepted file and identify the actual comparison beside the requested scope.
`DiffFileList`, `DiffFileHeader` and `DiffFilePath` share file presentation in `ui`;
portable state and Runtime reads remain with their existing owners.

Diff's local Refresh uses the shared Button's existing `pending` contract.
A pending read keeps the operation's keyboard place while refusing pointer,
Enter and Space activation. Escape remains available to the existing local
list return or host source return, after any open Tooltip closes. Completion
and failure require no compensating focus transition; a later outside choice
remains authoritative. No read, selection, comparison, abort, retry or shared
Button default changed. The checked portable ESM was regenerated from its source.

The native baseline, not a desktop screenshot alone, established the defect:
Enter on Refresh moved focus to the document, and completion left the next Tab
at the fixture sidebar. Exact installed Base UI 1.9.0 and its
[loading-state guidance](https://base-ui.com/react/components/button#loading-states)
initially led to `focusableWhenDisabled`. The subsequent native Skills task
exposed that this option prevents every non-Tab key, including the Escape that
the parent reader owns. Diff used the same option. Existing shared `pending`
pauses only the button's activation and preserves that navigation boundary.
HeroUI's local
`button/loading-state.tsx` retains named pending action identity; Flame retains
its own status copy and controls. ZCode's `useGitAutoRefresh.ts` distinguishes
background observation from explicit refresh; its debounce/watcher policy is
unrelated to this focus repair. ChatGPT's actual
`review-file-tree-side-pane-3e6c9e54ce58.js` separates pending, error, missing and
accepted review data and is interface code, not an exporter. None of those
references establishes native button focus behavior; actual Wails does. The
better-series focus/layout review keeps the existing typography, semantic
surfaces, Blue/Flame Orange rings and whole review/return task.

Usage applies the same distinction between query scope and presentation: the
report period and refresh precede source search and grouping. A pending or failed
period change visibly names the accepted period in the persistent control zone.
Filtering still changes only displayed buckets, never the report totals.
Usage Refresh now uses shared `pending` so a read does not remove the user's
keyboard position. Success, failure and explicit retry preserve that control;
later filter or Composer focus remains authoritative. Its scope, accepted report,
filter, grouping and Runtime request owner are unchanged.

Skills now separates three catalog tasks. Available rows use the shared
`RecordButton` to enter a named document directly; personal library rows show their
complete lifecycle information and Archive/Restore without a redundant disclosure;
proposal rows expand their actual evidence and retain the existing captured
confirmation. Escape cancels that local confirmation before page navigation and
returns focus to its action. Runtime command and uncertain-acknowledgement owners
remain unchanged.

Document navigation keeps the Available catalog, query and canonical refreshes
alive beneath the reader. Its own cancellable read and persistent name/compact
scope replace the catalog controls. Literal instructions use `DocumentBody` and
`RecordBody`; source path and revision are secondary disclosure. Back/Escape
restores the catalog position and source row, falling back to search if a canonical
read removed it. Background completion does not take a later focus choice. First
failures claim no retained data; same-document refresh failures identify the
accepted document. Document navigation does not recreate the Runtime subscription.
Diff file rows now consume the same `RecordButton` presentation without changing
their selection, comparison or return ownership.

Skills catalog and document Refresh now use shared `pending` for active reads.
Both remain focusable while refusing duplicate activation. Catalog refresh
retains its query and accepted rows; document refresh retains its named accepted
instructions and the existing error/retry path. Escape can retire the document's
read and restore the exact catalog row, while late completion stays retired.
An open Tooltip consumes its own Escape first. The catalog's independent
`disabled={Boolean(command)}` remains native disablement: an unresolved command
is still governed by its original command and recovery contract. This is a local
read-control repair, with no global Button behavior change or new focus state.

Provider rows retain one credential draft owner and one canonical mutation owner. `providers/ui/ProviderRow.tsx` now runs Save & Test through one existing `useAsyncFeedback` lease that starts at activation. The mutation latch and saving state remain local to the admitted update; `saveDraft` still settles only the submitted URL/key values. Ordinary Save/Clear keep their existing feedback policy. A retired Runtime generation remains ignored, current save failure prevents testing, and test failure uses its existing message. Editing does not submit the successor draft or cancel an already accepted canonical update. No new draft, generation cache, protocol or persisted representation was added.

Settings layout now keeps its responsive conditions in the same native page-zoom space as its content. `ui/atoms/vertical-tabs.tsx` uses 40rem for the existing navigation retreat, and `settings/kit/SettingRow.tsx` uses 35rem for its existing row retreat and control alignment. At the observed 16 px root these retain the previous 640/560 px thresholds; the 14/18 px interface font remains owned by appearance. `providers/ui/ProvidersPane.tsx` composes its model roles with the existing `SettingsGroup`, so each row queries the group's content width rather than the wider outer Panel. The credential list retains its separate `RecordGroup` layout. No measurement state, zoom mirror, native preference, draft or mutation owner was added.

The cross-page composition rule is now recorded in `DESIGN.md`: establish location
and data scope, show a useful summary, then expose the current object's content and
actions. Source review across the existing pages gives this task map:

| Page family                      | Task model to preserve                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| -------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Conversation and Tool evidence   | Read the answer, scan outcomes, expand supporting evidence, inspect material and return to its source.                                                                                                                            |
| File, Diff and subagent readers  | Locate an object, read it persistently, navigate related objects and unwind the actual entry path. A directory command is separate from returning to a narrative source.                                                          |
| Settings and resource management | Identify the selected scope/object before editing; keep current state and the primary command visible, with bounded forms and explicit cancellation. Memory, Skills and Schedules retain their existing command and scope owners. |
| Usage reports                    | Select the data period before local grouping/filtering; identify retained results by their accepted period.                                                                                                                       |
| Trajectory and diagnostics       | Scan a linear record summary and expand supporting evidence/provenance without turning every record into a new navigation destination.                                                                                            |

These are task-specific compositions of shared controls, not a requirement to turn
all pages into the same card, accordion or modal. The source review does not claim
that every unchanged page has received fresh native acceptance.

The empty Dock now has a clear title, supporting context and a fixed search field
above its scrolling groups. The directory and header picker use one `CatalogList`
for filtering, icons, open marks and keyboard selection, including searching by a
localized group name. Missing contributed icons keep a stable fallback column.
The directory is retained within its Session so inspection and return preserve
the query, results position and originating search control. Picker selections
retire their popup before entering the selected material; canceling restores the
trigger. The selection marker remains through Base UI's final focus settlement
so the trigger cannot steal focus back from the material. Full-view-to-Dock entry
uses the same inspection owner and returns to its named main reader.

The shared document focus rule now suppresses the browser's native outline on
`data-chrome-focus` controls. Their existing field edge or row state owns focus;
ordinary keyboard targets retain the global semantic ring. Native WebKit exposed
a second blue outline inside the catalog search field during keyboard navigation;
the final native review confirmed a single field edge and the ordinary semantic
ring on the Dock return button.

`SearchField` owns literal query defaults and local Escape clearing. Nonempty
editable queries clear through the existing `onClear` callback and retain input
focus, including when Escape starts on the clear action. Empty queries leave
enclosing navigation available. Native-only fields preserve the browser's default
clear rather than synthesizing input events. Explicitly handled keys and blocked
mutation states retain their previous owners. Actual portable filters and shortcut
search now provide the explicit clear callback and name; Settings no longer
duplicates this keyboard rule.

`ui/src/keyboard.ts` owns the shared `isComposing`/legacy keyCode 229 predicate.
Dock return, Diff/Skills local navigation and the former frontend helper's consumers
use it; the retired `src/lib/ime.ts` is removed. The stable code reader uses the
same predicate without changing its scoped literal Select All behavior. Recorded
output Find remains a bounded task: its owner captures Escape to close back to the
output, while Enter/Shift+Enter cycle matches outside composition.

`ui/src/reader-selection.ts` owns the shared local Select All guard and
whole-reader copy event. CodeBody retains its existing literal-region behavior.
Captured result readers supply one presented-row slice to rendering and copy;
line labels, line numbers and continuation controls are presentation, not copied
evidence. Preview copy includes only the presented excerpt, preserving real blank
lines instead of rendering padding. Expanded output copies the complete original
ANSI-stripped source, including offscreen lines and trailing newlines. Partial,
outside, collapsed, already-handled and editable-descendant copies stay native.
The browser range is the only selection owner. Mutable result and virtual-output
rows sit beneath one stable child so reconciliation cannot shorten its boundary
offsets; the existing viewport, focus, scroll and measurement owners stay in place.

Recorded result emptiness now comes from the captured rows, before local Find.
Empty hit/web-result arrays say that no results were returned, including while a
query is open. Only a filter hiding existing rows offers Close find to recover
the original evidence. Clearing and closing retain their existing field/reader
focus destinations, and explicit Copy still writes the captured source. An empty
change receipt says that no changes were recorded instead of displaying an empty
table. Literal blank source rows remain content. These are read-only projections;
the Tool row still owns identity, status and errors, and no retry or replay is
introduced. `plugins/tool-results/component/component.tsx` and its rebuilt ESM
retain the same shape selectors, captured context and resource lifecycle.

Read-only research compared HeroUI's actual table `empty-state.tsx` demo with
ChatGPT's `compact-empty-state-7787ff604ab1.js`, an actual React interface component
with separate title, description and optional actions. These establish empty-state
composition; Flame's captured-result contract supplies outcome and replay policy.
ZCode's actual
`ToolCallBlocks/renderers/search.tsx` keeps search identity/status in the summary;
its non-expandable result policy was not copied. Better-writing's distinction
between original emptiness and filtered recovery, with existing layout,
typography and semantic Metadata, fits Flame's inline evidence task without a
new dialog, color system or state owner.

Segmented tracks use the existing compact navigation shape step. Their items and
moving backplate share the track radius minus its actual padding; there is no
track border to subtract. This leaves room for the radius preference to change
short Tab items visibly. The visual-style owner and its first-paint stylesheet
mirror publish the same mapping. Preset values, the shared shape ladder, the
curve feature query and explicitly round controls retain their previous owners.

Schedules keeps its catalog/query/page mounted beneath a bounded Add/Edit task
and a separate named deletion alert. Shared `ModalDialog` owns the header,
scrolling body, persistent footer, focus trap and nested popup priorities.
`packages/ui/src/modal-dialog.tsx` now establishes a modal stacking context so shared
menus/selects remain above its panel. Its native `finalFocus` and `dismissible`
contracts avoid competing focus cleanup; composition Escape includes legacy
keyCode 229 protection.

`packages/ui/src/content-visibility.tsx` owns the inherited visibility fact and
optional host `fallbackFocus` ref. `ModalDialog` resets visibility only for its
open Portal task; a hidden provider inside that task can still retire its own
popup. Its existing Base UI final-focus resolver checks explicit targets at
close time and substitutes the host ref only when the backing owner is hidden.
`useInspectionFocus` derives that ref from the existing main/Dock/Conversation
presentation, and `ChatPanel` passes it into each retained scope. Base UI keeps
its normal first-tabbable-descendant behavior for a returned region. No new
navigation state, event handler, focus retry or business transition was added.

`plugins/schedules/component/useSchedules.ts` owns the captured return target and keeps
command progress, failure and original-key reconciliation in the persistent
action zone. Cancellation returns to the actual available record/Add/Templates
entry. An absent or read-disabled record falls back to retained search; accepted
edits/deletion also return there during the canonical refresh. Creation and
cancellation return to Add/Templates even while that read is pending. A later
refresh does not steal focus. Accepted revisions, model identity, Runtime
commands, uncertain settlement/retry and read cancellation retain their previous
owners. This choice does not turn other management forms into modals.

Schedules catalog reads now distinguish unavailable data from an accepted empty
page. `plugins/schedules/component/component.tsx` derives the empty result from
the existing accepted page, retaining its records, counts or filtered empty state
during another read. A first failure names the read and Refresh recovery without
claiming that no schedules exist or that an unaccepted page was retained. A later
failure explicitly labels the accepted page. Refresh uses shared `pending` while
the independent unresolved-command guard remains natively disabled. Query,
status, surviving disclosure instances, background subscription, cursor and
command ownership remain unchanged; no focus effect or new read state was added.

Memory authoring remains one local non-modal task over its retained catalog.
`plugins/memory/src/component.tsx` shows the captured scope and stable record ID
in edit/deletion tasks. Each original command captures its action, target and
editor/record origin. Draft progress, refusal and original-key reconciliation
stay beside form actions; independent record feedback stays with Saved memory
without erasing a draft's refusal. Confirmed form results move to that catalog
context, including when no page could be accepted. New forms clear prior form
feedback and changing scope retires the previous scope's notices.

Only the original promise settles the task. Its subsequent canonical read has a
separate lifetime/loading state; it cannot revoke a known result or prolong the
command guard. No optimistic memory record or new receipt owner is introduced.
Local Escape cancels the current task after literal search and nested choices
have handled their key; composition leaves it intact and an unresolved command
consumes it without discarding the task. `RecordForm` still owns focus retirement.
The Memory resolver rejects a disconnected or read-disabled opener and uses
retained search. A later read never reclaims a subsequent focus choice.

Plugin management places Installed packages after installation and before
Provider selections, retaining the latter's existing provider/command owners.
`settings/plugins-pane/ui/PackageManager.tsx` captures name and optional version
with the existing command target when an editor opens. Live catalog replacement
cannot relabel an editor that still submits the captured installation/digest.
Configure exposes the reviewed release digest and labels JSON input changes;
Stage labels the Runtime path, uses Stage update as its completion action and
explains the separate selection step. The digest is an admission identity, not
a claim that compatible installation configuration cannot survive selection.
Eight locales replace the former mixed JSON-or-path label. The existing
`TextEditorDialog` only forwards optional description content to `ModalDialog`;
shared wrapping, scrolling, focus, pending fields and submission retain their
owners. No backend-only page, schema form, command or protocol was added.

Saved usage reports now separate the retained archive from a named reading view.
`plugins/usagereports/component/component.tsx` keeps the capture period, archive rows,
source button and actual ancestor scroll positions while a report is open. Its
existing `Panel` controls own Back, Refresh and the report's period/observation
identity. Entry resets the pane to the top and focuses the heading only while
that intent still owns focus; later data acceptance does not reclaim it. Back
restores the available source or falls back to Refresh archive.

Refresh report now uses the shared Button's existing `pending` contract. Its
active read preserves keyboard position while refusing repeated activation;
success, failure and explicit retry need no compensating focus effect. Back
still retires the report read and restores the original archive row, period and
scroll positions; a late result cannot reopen the report. Later focus choices
remain authoritative. Archive refresh, capture, removal and original-command
recovery keep their existing semantics.

Initial archive loading, failed reads and an accepted empty archive are distinct.
Failed report refresh keeps the accepted report and a reachable retry. Reading
shows the actual lower bound and rolling 24-hour/7-day meaning; raw report ID and
UTC times remain available in the exact-identity disclosure. Bigint/JSON-number
formatting, recorded zero, missing prices and core ownership are unchanged.

Removal is a named shared `ModalDialog` decision with Cancel initially focused.
Opening, Cancel and Escape admit no command. Confirmation submits the captured
report ID once; the pending action says Removing report and retains the modal
boundary. Cancellation returns to Remove, while accepted removal returns to the
archive's available refresh action. Original-command recover/retire and binding
retirement remain with the public SDK. A recovered old deletion may close the
current report only when fresh canonical membership says it is gone; failed
membership reads preserve it. Known deletion also retains its captured target.
No target is guessed from SDK handles and no command journal was added.

Explicitly opening another report retires old operation feedback. Original
removal recovery names the original operation rather than implying that the
currently viewed report was removed. Successful capture still opens its new
report when its initiating focus remains unclaimed; otherwise it names the saved
copy in archive feedback. The published ESM was rebuilt. Existing Runtime
installations require explicit reinstallation and selection of the new package
digest; a source/resource rebuild does not update an admitted installation.

## Global Composer focus and retained material

The existing `composer.focus` command belongs to the installed workbench. Its ID,
localized label, Mod-L binding and stored shortcut-override identity are unchanged.
The plugin installation owns one React ref to `ChatPanel`'s small imperative
handle; no global handler registry was added. The obsolete single-export panel
index has no consumers and is removed. Other default commands retain their owner.

`useInspectionFocus` handles the explicit request before its generic presentation
focus. A visible Composer receives ordinary local input focus without navigation.
For a hidden conversation, one ref-owned request captures the Session, active
element, inspection and expected Dock location. An immutable token ensures a
commit even when later navigation coalesces back to the old route. Delivery is
one-shot and requires a visible conversation, unchanged scope and still-unclaimed
focus. A later control/region, replacement Session or superseding navigation
retires the request; unmounting retains no deferred callback. An active dialog
keeps its task boundary. No timeout, retry, forced flush or region fallback was
added.

Workspace `selectChat` accepts an explicit preserve-Dock or hide-Dock intent in
one existing navigation operation. A narrow inspection can therefore reveal the
conversation without the inspection immediately projecting full width again.
The view and optional Dock change form one history entry; stored tabs, queries,
file targets and inspection ancestry remain with their existing owners. Ordinary
selection, Close and notification callers keep the default preserve policy.

Manual full-width reopening also goes through the existing Workspace `showDock`
owner. Its main presentation uses the active location or the existing
`dockTabToShow` memory policy, with the catalog for an empty Dock. The host no
longer guesses the first tab or a default Files view. This completes explicit
inspection → focus/continue drafting → reopen the same material, as well as
history Back. The established low-level Composer focus, captured acceptance
return, draft and submit ports remain unchanged; asynchronous acceptance does not
gain this explicit navigation behavior. No Runtime or stored schema changed.

The read-only ZCode pane-focus references above inform the visible-owner policy.
Flame's actual navigation/history, registered input target and retained-view
lifetimes supply the implementation contracts. Existing Base UI modal semantics
and better-series focus/layout guidance preserve bounded tasks, source returns,
typography, semantic colors and the approved visual system.

## Search selection and task handoff

`src/ui/atoms/search-overlay.tsx` owns the end of the bounded search task before
handing its selected action to a destination. Pointer and Enter selection capture
one callback and request controlled closure. Base UI's close completion publishes
an immutable token; a committed effect consumes the callback from its sole ref
owner after modal isolation cleanup. The retained intent prevents a later
primitive return-focus job from overriding the selected destination. Repeated
activation during closing cannot dispatch the choice twice, and unmounting
retires a choice that has not been delivered.

If the closing surface still owns focus, the surviving opener is restored before
the action runs. A local command such as Toggle theme therefore retains context,
and an action can intentionally focus a destination or open another search task
from that same origin. Later outside focus remains authoritative for cancellation
and non-focusing actions. Ordinary dismissal executes no choice. CommandMenu and
SessionSearch retain only their existing command/session callbacks; the shared
surface now owns their close request. Query/highlight resets, matching, row
announcements, keyboard/IME handling and visual tokens retain their established
contracts. Business command, Session, navigation and Runtime owners are unchanged.

Read-only research inspected ZCode's actual
`command-center/CommandCenterDialog.tsx` and
`quickpick/QuickPickCommandDialog.tsx`: both request closure before handing
the selected command or task to its owner. Their immediate callbacks are task-model
evidence, not proof of Wails focus ordering. Installed Base UI 1.9.0
`FloatingFocusManager.mjs` shows its queued return focus and the explicit-target
exception to its outside-focus guard; `popupStoreUtils.mjs` and
`useUnmountAfterClose.mjs` establish the close-completion/commit distinction.
Flame's existing CatalogPicker supplies a local one-shot handoff precedent, while
the search surface also needs a return for non-focusing commands. Better-series
focus and layout guidance retains the existing modal, semantic surfaces, query
field, reading/list rhythm and Orange/custom-blue focus roles. Existing Radix
Dialog and HeroUI modal references inform the bounded task model; static Ace
artwork supplies no keyboard evidence.

## Composer model catalog recovery

`src/features/composer/ui/ModelPicker.tsx` now treats catalog availability separately
from resolution of the current model. When a nonempty catalog cannot resolve the
Session's exact selection, the existing localized Switch model action and neutral
server glyph remain available. Provider and recent groups still contain only
known catalog entries, with no row marked as current. Loading, empty and failed
catalog branches retain their existing behavior.

Integration retains the already-authored primary `main` contract from
`02ac171c8`: an explicit preference carries its provider/model identity even when
catalog metadata is unavailable, and sending reads that preference directly.
Its owner/callers/tests and localized unavailable hint are included as an exact
frontend dependency. Such a preference still displays its original identity,
provider and unavailable hint, including an empty catalog. The neutral action is
reserved for absence of a resolved identity. Choosing a replacement remains an
explicit user decision; this presentation repair does not advance the selection
on its own.

The existing selection resolver still reports unknown when a durable Session's
model is absent, its summary is pending, or a new Session has no Runtime default.
Opening, searching and cancelling the picker do not choose another model or write
a preference. An explicit choice still writes the exact provider/model pair,
remembers that item and keeps a supported prior effort or the selected model's
own default. Once resolved, the trigger, provider scope and current-row treatment
retain their established behavior. An open search also survives loss of the
current selection instead of unmounting with the old loading placeholder.
No new state owner, fallback model, effect, focus workaround, command or wire
contract was introduced.

Read-only research inspected ZCode's actual
`chat-input-toolbar/modelSelection.ts`: trigger display and catalog membership
are distinct concerns. Its `useModelSelectionView.ts` also distinguishes retained
catalog data from a resolved effective selection; its service and retry machinery
were not copied. HeroUI's local `select/controlled.tsx` keeps a nullable controlled
value and a usable placeholder, while `popover/basic.tsx` keeps the bounded task
with its trigger. These support the task model rather than a replacement control
system. Better-writing guidance favors the existing localized action over an
indefinite loading signal; better-accessibility/layout review retains Base UI's
keyboard/focus behavior, shared chip sizing, semantic surfaces and Orange/custom
accent roles. Static Ace references supply no selection or focus evidence.

## Captured Session naming task

The existing Session-row action opens a bounded naming task over the retained
workspace. `RenameSession` in `plugins/trajectory/component/component.tsx` now
uses the shared TextField and Button pending contracts rather than native busy
disablement. The exact submitted title stays read-only and focusable; Save says
`Saving title…` and original-command reconciliation says
`Checking original command…`, with status semantics and `aria-busy`. Repeated
activation remains refused by the existing controls and command admission guard.
The saved or unknown title remains reviewable and read-only. Definitive refusal
restores editing without replacing the draft or moving later user-chosen focus.

The existing captured Session ID/revision, exact input, mutation handle/key,
canonical response, cancellation and lifetime owners are unchanged. This adds
no command, timer, focus restoration policy or competing submission state. The
Trajectory reader before this action is byte-identical to the Run-filter
milestone. The shared UI and host dialog remain unchanged.

Read-only HeroUI `button/loading.tsx` and `button/loading-state.tsx` demonstrate
explicit pending action text; their demo timer is not copied. Flame's actual
TextField pending contract and Button regression coverage supply the behavior.
The better-accessibility and better-writing review keeps the named operation,
submitted object and recovery visible in the existing modal task, with current
semantic surfaces, type, Orange/custom accents and host return policy.

## Trajectory page scope and reading continuity

`plugins/trajectory/component/component.tsx` keeps the accepted cursor chain and
captured descendant scope as the sole page identity. Refresh rereads those values;
it no longer silently returns an older-page reader to the newest page. Initial
entry still negotiates scope. The visible status names the accepted page and
record count, with singular/plural wording and an explicit last-accepted label
after failure. Counts and filters remain local to that page; opaque cursors do
not establish a total page count.

Refresh and Newer/Older use the shared Button's existing `pending` contract.
Native disablement represents only real first/last boundaries. One transient
navigation origin is consumed after a successful accepted-page commit: the
existing summary receives focus only while the initiating action or unclaimed
document body owns it. Initial reads, refresh, failures, retired reads and later
filter/Composer focus choices do not move it. The existing Panel owns revealing
that focused body below its measured sticky controls; no local scroll mechanism,
timer, competing page state or shared primitive change was needed. Filtering and
same-page refresh preserve surviving record disclosure instances.

The Run selection now remains the one authoritative filter through page changes
and refreshes. A selected Run absent from the accepted page stays in the option
collection and produces an empty match; it no longer temporarily substitutes
All Runs. Only the currently selected missing value is retained, without a cache
of past Runs or another state transition. The empty-string Session only identity
keeps the same rule. Explicit All Runs remains an explicit choice. Empty-match
guidance names All Runs alongside the existing text/type recovery controls.

Read-only ZCode `EnabledStatusFilterSelect.tsx` supplies one controlled value to
its filter; `automationStatusFilter.ts` places an intentional reset at its actual
tab transition. Flame's cursor navigation changes the accepted page within the
same reading task, so no second filter policy belongs in that projection.
better-writing's truthful scope and recoverable-empty guidance applies here;
existing shared Select, Panel, focus and semantic surface owners are unchanged.

| Severity | Location                                     | Before                                                                          | After                                                                                                 | Why                                                                   |
| -------- | -------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| HIGH     | `plugins/trajectory/component/component.tsx` | A page without the selected Run silently showed All Runs and unrelated records. | The selected Run remains visible and filters the accepted page; guidance names the explicit recovery. | Accepted material must not independently widen a user-selected scope. |

## Shortcut recording and activation

`apps/web/src/lib/combo.ts` now includes the canonical `space` / physical `Space`
pair in its existing dispatch-code map. Its inverse captures the same key;
normalization, labels, conflict lookup and tinykeys dispatch consume that one
representation. Previously bare Space normalized to an empty key and left the
recorder open, while modified Space saved a modifier-only binding that could not
activate the command. Existing event suppression, recording ownership, Tab,
Escape, input policy, conflicts, persistence and reset retain their owners.

ZCode's actual `settings/useShortcutRecording.ts`, shortcut bindings and labels
were inspected for capture identity and bounded cancellation. Its modifier and
bare-key policy differs from Flame's and was not copied. ChatGPT's actual
`keyboard-shortcuts-settings-10f7592e9ebf.js` supplies command identity, capture,
conflict and cancellation callbacks, rather than exporter behavior. The
[UI Events code specification](https://www.w3.org/TR/uievents-code/#key-alphanumeric-writing-system)
identifies `Space`; the installed tinykeys matcher accepts exact physical codes
or case-normalized logical key strings, while spaces delimit sequences. Flame
uses its existing physical-key policy and semantic control/type/color tokens.
The better-series review preserves the whole change → capture → activate → reset
path without adding state, palette values, a component system or geometry.

Composer's logical-key projection has different modifier semantics, and none of
its six current bindings uses Space. It remains unchanged; adding such a binding
would require reviewing that owner's actual contract. No protocol, dependency or
preference-schema migration is needed. A previously saved modifier-only override
cannot reveal its lost intended key; reset and record it again rather than guess.

## File browsing and keyboard continuity

`apps/web/src/features/workspace/ui/files/FileTree.tsx` retains one stored cursor:
the last row that actually received focus. Its visible keyboard entry is derived
through the currently rendered directory responses. A surviving row keeps the
entry; a hidden or removed descendant uses its nearest visible ancestor; an
absent root uses the first remaining root. Loading and error states use the same
visibility conditions as `DataView`. Collapsing a branch while working elsewhere
keeps that later control's focus and lets the original cursor resume if the
branch reopens without another row receiving focus.

The tree root owns removal focus through its private ref and stable callbacks.
A row reports its retirement before detachment; the replacement only reclaims
focus when that removed row owned it and the document body still has it. Child
rows do not mutate a borrowed ref. Existing preview/back, selected-file identity,
expanded-directory memory, query lifetimes and Runtime filesystem ownership stay
with their existing owners. Root loading replaces the entire tree; this change
guarantees a reachable entry on remount, not automatic focus across that separate
loading boundary.

`apps/web/src/ui/atoms/tree.tsx` uses the existing `data-focus-proxy` rule.
Actual Wails exposed a pointer-to-arrow path where the active row moved without
WebKit making it `:focus-visible` until a later Tab. The proxy gives that same
row the shared focus ring as soon as the existing document modality owner sees
keyboard navigation. It adds no event listener, palette, focus state or outline
value; pointer focus still follows the established suppression policy.

The existing workspace fixture now names its root `apps`, matching the file paths
after the repository layout change. Its ordinary browser expectations follow
that corrected identity; no browser suite was started. Native refresh controls
and theme links are temporary build transforms, not shipped debug behavior.

Reference review inspected ZCode's `workspace-file-tree/WorkspaceFileTree.tsx`,
`WorkspaceFileTreeList.tsx` and `WorkspaceFileTreeRowView.tsx` for row activation,
keyboard access and selected-object handling. ChatGPT's actual
`review-file-tree-side-pane-3e6c9e54ce58.js` separates selected file, selected root
and pane width; it is interface code, not an exporter. Flame keeps its existing
roving tree rather than copying either navigation system. HeroUI's local Tabs
and Popover demos, the linked Radix focus and semantic color references above,
and the Ace overviews plus full-size Alpaca tool and Marketer workspace images
inform persistent identity, task grouping and quiet supporting detail. Static
artwork supplies no keyboard evidence. The six better-series domains apply to
this file-browse/read/return flow; unchanged colors, typography and control
surfaces retain their existing owners.

## Dock tab task continuity

`apps/web/src/ui/agent/context-dock.tsx` keeps tab selection and close/reorder
commands with their existing host owners. Its local post-action focus intent now
captures the actual document origin and the affected tab's live popup. Only a tab,
that popup or an unclaimed body can initiate settlement. The existing frame
boundary respects later focus, including another tab, and ignores disconnected
destinations. A closing popup can still finish its own focus entry before that
frame without being mistaken for an unrelated choice. Popup identity is derived
from the rendered tab ID, without a shared ref competing across menu lifetimes.

The existing tab list prevents only middle-button mousedown's focus transfer;
primary pressing, Base UI activation, overflow and drag/drop retain their owners.
Middle-closing a background tab therefore keeps a surviving Composer or reader
control and its text/selection context. A removed focused tab settles on the
surviving active tab, or the available header return/Browse action when none
remains. Delete/Backspace also respect an already consumed key and the existing
shared `isImeKey` contract, including legacy 229. No new tab state, global listener,
timer, geometry, theme or command policy was introduced.

Read-only research inspected ZCode's actual `SidePaneTabTrigger.tsx` and
`AnimatedSidePanePanel.tsx` for background closure and contextual actions. Its
custom-div/all-pointer policy was not copied into Flame's native Base UI buttons.
Installed Base UI 1.9.0 explicitly permits secondary-press focus without
selection, which is a different fact from continued authoring focus. HeroUI's
local overflow Tabs example and prior Radix tab/focus references inform the
composite and overflow model rather than a new close lifecycle. ChatGPT's actual
side-panel source remains evidence for separate material identity; it is not
native close-focus proof. Better-series review retains current type, semantic
surfaces, Orange/custom-blue focus and the same workbench composition. Static
Ace references supply no keyboard evidence.

## Retained workspace and result views

The native Memory query reset had two host causes. Activity hide disconnected
`RenderingComponent`'s external-store subscription, ending the captured context;
composition Effect cleanup also retired resolved descendants. Main and Dock
placements separately rendered the same view. Preserving only a search value
would have left drafts and original mutation retry closures attached to retired
contexts.

`ChatPanel.tsx` now renders one keyed body per exact Session/view ID. Its existing
Dock tabs, active main view and distinct main sources on the inspection chain
supply retention; there is no second state cache. A same-view return shortcut
cannot keep a closed moved tab alive. `PanelHost` changes only its context value
through the optional `hosted` prop, preserving full-pane and Dock density without
moving children. The stable Dock container is neutral and non-focusable in main
mode; the named body and persistent window header keep their roles, and main
content does not inherit Dock containment/clipping.

Conversation results and retained delegated readers use the same mounted-tree
policy. `packages/ui/src/content-visibility.tsx` projects inherited presentation visibility
without state, cancellation or I/O. `MessageStream` preserves the public follow
library's two DOM wrappers and viewport class, attaching its measuring refs only
while visible. `ChatStream` suspends only its Composer geometry observer, and
`SubagentTranscript` combines ancestor and local visibility. This matters in real
WebKit: retained hidden readers otherwise measured zero and jumped to the bottom.
No scroll-position cache or replacement business lifetime was added.

Native page zoom while a retained reader is hidden has a separate limit. On
macOS 26.5, changing 150% to 200% while its ancestor is `hidden` changed a
777,329 CSS-pixel position to 582,997 on return. A plain HTML scroller reproduced
the exact change without React, virtualization, ResizeObserver or application
scroll handlers. Ordinary hide/show at unchanged zoom retained 777,329, and visible
zoom retained that same position. This is not evidence of another component
lifetime or virtual-window defect. The [WebKit scroll-area implementation](https://github.com/WebKit/WebKit/blob/main/Source/WebCore/rendering/RenderLayerScrollableArea.cpp)
saves and restores a layer position; [the DOM scroll getter](https://github.com/WebKit/WebKit/blob/main/Source/WebCore/dom/Element.cpp)
removes the current zoom. A saved layer offset restored under a different scale
is consistent with the observed 150/200 ratio. That is a source-based explanation,
not identification of the exact installed system WebKit revision. Keep this
limitation explicit rather than adding a competing application scroll cache or
keeping every hidden workspace laid out solely for this edge case.

`useInspectionFocus.ts` gives a removed layout trigger's focus to the presented
main body, visible Dock or conversation only when focus fell to the document
body. Existing explicit source returns and later outside focus keep priority.
Actual tab removal, main-only replacement, exact Session/target replacement and
resource/contribution retirement retain their cancellation behavior. Runtime,
receipts, bindings, SDK and portable component sources are unchanged.

Reference evidence: ZCode's read-only `AnimatedSidePanePanel.tsx` retains actual
tab content with `forceMount` plus inactive hiding and explicit visibility for
owned side-pane work. ChatGPT's actual `ReviewThreadSidePanelTab` separates its
review state from the portal target; it is not export-renderer evidence. The
[React Activity contract](https://react.dev/reference/react/Activity) documents
Effect cleanup on hide; [Radix Tabs](https://www.radix-ui.com/primitives/docs/components/tabs)
provides the semantic reference for persistent tab content. Flame retains its
Base UI/StyleX owners. The better-layout/accessibility review drove instance,
measurement, landmark and focus boundaries; existing typography, writing and
semantic color owners were checked in both native themes without a palette or
prose redesign.

## Transcript controls and floating navigation

The main transcript's focus scroll padding now reserves the Composer overlay
plus the larger of its existing fade or the floating Jump control, its gap and
the shared control clearance. The original fade-only allowance could put a
focused result-search outline behind that button at 200%. This is an observed
overlap; the focused control was not wholly hidden, so no whole-focus-obscured
WCAG failure is claimed.

by both `JumpToBottomButton.tsx` and `.msg-scroll-viewport` in `globals.css`.
`SubagentsPanel.tsx` supplies its existing larger gap on the reader and reserves
the corresponding space on its own scroller. Both use the shared medium control
height and control gap. Actual content padding, Composer dimensions, scroll
height, follow policy and result controls retain their owners; no new state,
observer, event handler or imperative scrolling compensates for the geometry.

The read-only ZCode `ToolCallBody`/`ExecuteOutput` inspection separates record
identity and bounded output. The better-layout, better-typography and
better-accessibility review applies that task distinction here: navigation must
leave the current reading control visible. Captured result URLs and paths remain
literal historical output. This change does not add file/navigation commands or
reinterpret recorded material as current workspace data.

Latest Runtime receipts require explicit `added`/`removed` values. Six captured
patch receipts in `visual/agentSessionSnapshots.ts` now retain zero values for
their abbreviated, unparseable patch inputs, matching the Runtime presenter.
The existing contract test exposed the omission. No frontend patch grammar,
inferred count or permissive result fallback was introduced.

## Conversation reading axis ownership

Rendered conversation prose now reads on a single owned measure wherever a
`MessageTurn` is laid out. The reading axis (`readingColumn.box`/`gutter`) moved
from `shell/workbench/panel/readingColumn.ts` to its logical home beside the
renderer at `features/chat/message/ui/readingColumn.ts`, re-exported through
`features/chat/public/message/rendering`. The composer-overlay clearance and its
`--composer-overlay` property split into `shell/workbench/panel/composerOverlay.ts`,
since those belong to the shell that owns the floating composer, not to message
rendering. Shell consumers (`MessageStream`, `ChatStream`, `FloatingComposer`)
import the axis through the chat public surface — the same direction by which the
shell already consumes `MessageTurn`.

The earlier move repaired the dependency direction but left an incomplete
composition: `SubagentsPanel` used the capped box without its gutter. Actual
Wails showed an 808 px inner body where the main reader had 768 px; padding on
the outside scroller did not repair that contract. The existing transcript div
now composes both `readingColumn.box` and `readingColumn.gutter`. Its local
`--reading-gutter` projects the existing `--layout-compact-gutter`, preserving the
narrow Dock's inset. The overview list keeps that same token on its scroller;
the transcript no longer adds a second outer inset. Shared reading tokens and
the existing 640 px gutter breakpoint remain authoritative.

Final Wails acceptance qualified the complete composition in English/light/14 px/
Flame Orange and Chinese/dark/18 px/custom blue. The full-pane body uses the
shared 768 px inner cap at Actual Size, and a native window resize enters the
existing wide-content band without losing its right gutter. Moving the selected
third fanout task into a 473 px Dock retains identity and the compact inset.
A separate 523→725 px Dock drag, 200% native zoom, actual narrow window and
task → file → task → conversation path retain the original source focus and
exact Chinese Composer draft. The fixture sidebar was explicitly collapsed for
zoomed reading; its static layout is not production navigation evidence.

Reference basis: ChatGPT's actual
`deob/webview/assets/subagent-panel-3efa108a843d.js` centers a full-width body on
the shared thread cap. ZCode's actual conversation/message components keep

## Empty, error and unsupported state ownership

The empty/error/unsupported distinction is now owned once. `EmptyState`
(`packages/ui`) gained a `tone` that colors the icon mark through the existing
`Tone` vocabulary, so a failure reads as a failure instead of relying on each
caller to remember an alert glyph. `IconMark` maps the tone to the shared badge
surfaces; its default stays the accent mark, so existing empties are unchanged.
`DataView` renders its error branch with `tone="negative"` and its unsupported
branch with `tone="warning"`, which covers every list screen in one place.

Four sites that bypassed `DataView` and conflated states were corrected:
`SubagentsPanel`'s unavailable run and `ApprovalsPane`'s load failure were
rendering an error as a neutral empty — the same icon as "nothing here" and no
retry — and now use the alert glyph with the negative tone, with Approvals
offering a retry. `HooksPane` dropped its hand-rolled unsupported branch and
routes that state through `DataView`'s `unsupported` config, removing the
duplicate. `ToolControls` uses the shared recovery surface; its local titles now
name tool visibility, auto-approve rules and the tools list so their independent
retries remain understandable.

The original shared-tone verification rebuilt `@flame/ui`, passed its 74 tests
and the then-current 3,069-test Web suite. Current native acceptance below
qualifies negative failure, warning unsupported and ordinary empty presentation
in the Approvals page. The MCP follow-up below qualifies its named recovery
states. The earlier subagent-unavailable path is not newly qualified here.

## Wide-content breakout in the reading band

Actual Wails on `955cfb690` exposed the missing boundary in the original broad
`.md` descendant rule. Expanded user code and images escaped the narrower user
bubble; quote, list and Callout code borrowed the conversation pane's width and
put actions behind the Dock or outside their local surface. The width formula
was not the owner of eligibility.

`BlockRenderer` now marks its existing text wrapper with
`data-slot="message-text"`. `markdown.css` grants breakout only through the
observed outer assistant text chain:
`.msg-content:not([data-user-message-bubble]) > [data-block-anchor] > [data-slot="message-text"] > .md`.
Only direct code, media, Mermaid frames and display math inside a direct paragraph
take the wide width. Direct table containers take only `max-width`, preserving
intrinsic narrow tables. User bubbles, nested Markdown, list/quote/Callout content,
reasoning and NarrativeWave text keep their local boundary. No wrapper, clipping,
measurement, new state or event workaround was added.

The existing 640–1151.98 px `conversation` container band, left-anchored growth,
56rem cap and width formula remain:
`max(100%, min(var(--wide-column-max), 100cqi - max(0px, (100cqi - var(--reading-column-max)) / 2) - 2 * var(--reading-gutter-wide)))`.
The complete subagent column composition above makes that geometry apply to the
same owned axis in a full pane and a widened Dock. Ordinary code, table and media
scrolling, selection, typography, radius, theme and Runtime owners are unchanged.
The better-layout, better-typography and better-accessibility guidance informs
stable grouping, reading measure and reachable local operations; it does not
justify a second component system or global theme change.

Native acceptance used the production Wails v3 host with isolated data and
canonical captured messages augmented only with code, table, wide SVG and nested
evidence. Before the subagent gutter correction, English/light/14 px/Orange
qualified bounded expanded user code/image (including the image's END), nested
evidence, wrap and keyboard Copy acknowledgement, file/source return and the
exact mixed-language draft. An actual window resize qualified outer code growing
beyond prose. After the final composition, renewed English full-pane acceptance
showed the shared 768 px inner cap; resizing the native capture from 2878×1798 to
2678×1798 grew the outer code to its 896 px cap with a 20 px right gutter.
Selected-task → file → task restores the exact source link; moving that task
into the 473 px Dock keeps the same task and local inset. Final Chinese/dark/
18 px/blue acceptance qualified 523→725 px pointer resizing, 20 successful
native Zoom In commands (200%), a 2632×1798 window, local code wrapping,
Tab → Copy with visible focus/acknowledgement, file → task → original third
fanout return, and the exact `子任务阅读草稿 保留` draft. Actual Size was restored
before exit. These observations qualify the exercised layouts; a visibly present
turn-navigation rail, all possible transitions and other native platforms were
