### Native math reading and input qualification

The earlier repeated-Right probe showed offset zero in both the changed band
(659 px viewport) and the unchanged outside-band control (768 px viewport),
despite a focused 870 px expression and trusted, unprevented input. Its missing
fact was the document selection. A follow-up on delivered base `c6e37ee16`
compared plain HTML div/span, a table and the canonical KaTeX objective inside
the same production Wails host. Passive text receipts recorded focus, selection,
offsets and input, without changing selection, scrolling or event defaults.

In the native control, Tab focused the plain scroller at offset zero with no
selection. Right then placed a collapsed document caret at its trailing END and
revealed it at offset 566; Left moved that caret by a character, retaining the
same offset until it crossed back to the leading text. Four further Left keys
returned offset zero. Span, table and KaTeX controls also exposed their trailing
content through native text navigation. The initial width-only div/span controls
had all their text in the visible half and showed no offset change; these were
not representative evidence of an inaccessible trailing text result. Advancing
frame callbacks, visible/focused document state and a separate text-field caret
check exclude the earlier hidden/fullscreen initialization condition in these
specific observations. This is an observation of native selection behavior,
not a claim about a particular platform preference or every scroll input.

The real production math fixture then received the same selection observations,
with its canonical messages unchanged. English/light/14 px/Orange at Actual Size
showed the first term, matrix and tall fraction. From the exact Composer draft
`Formula reading draft 保留`, five Shift+Tab keys reached Poor → Good →
Regenerate → Copy message → Mathematical expression without activating actions.
The first Right placed a caret at offset zero in the following explanatory
paragraph; it did not pan the focused math. One Left returned the caret into
the rendered expression's final `)`, producing a trusted scroll event and
offset 268 in the 602 px viewport over 870 px content. The actual final `(1)`
tag was visible within the reader. Five Tab keys returned through the existing
actions to Composer, retaining the exact draft. This closes the earlier
unqualified final-tag/task-return path for this scenario. It does not qualify
continuous arrow panning, a faster reverse path to the first term, different
typography/zoom or physical horizontal wheel input. CUA horizontal scrolling
still produced no observed DOM wheel event, including in the bare control;
absence of that delivery is not a product wheel-failure verdict.

No product source, native preference, ARIA, selection owner or keyboard/scroll
fallback changed. The shared `useScrollReach`, `MarkdownMath` and browser default
remain the owners. The better-accessibility guidance favors preserving native
text interaction until a complete task demonstrates a defect. Thirty-five
product/host/fixture/manifest inputs and the private production harness inputs
remained frozen through acceptance. The native control builds passed, Web staging
passed (5.81 s), the production-fixture Wails build passed (5.24 s), math rendering/
trust tests passed (4 tests, 2.23 s) and scroll-reach contracts passed (9 tests,
1.15 s). Full Web/native aggregate checks were not repeated for this documentation
qualification; the unchanged product revision retains the preceding batch's
explicit pass/failure limits above. No browser suite or benchmark was run.

All six follow-up native PIDs exited. The exact app was unregistered; its private

## Tool argument copy outcomes

The built-in Copy arguments action now acknowledges an accepted clipboard write
and gives localized recovery guidance when the Clipboard API refuses it. The
notice names the existing Arguments section as the manual-copy route. Notices
contain neither captured material nor system error text; the row's concise name,
Tooltip, summary description and focus retain their owners.

Shared `copyText` owns the accepted-write boolean; `lib/clipboard.writeToClipboard`
projects it as an opt-in transient acknowledgement and returns it unchanged.
Its new optional `failureLabel` reports only a requested failure; existing
callers retain their prior behavior. `tools/toolMeta` supplies both
labels through that helper and resolves translations when the action runs.
The existing application action transfers the exact argument string. Shared
clipboard primitives, extension action contracts, command lifecycle, Runtime,
reader selection and Toaster presentation remain unchanged. Both new keys are
complete across eight locales and reuse the current section-name translation.

Flame's existing BrandPane checks the same copy result and names a manual-copy
recovery. Read-only ZCode `SnippetCopyButton` handles both absent Clipboard API
and rejected writes through its error callback. These inform truthful outcomes;
Flame keeps its existing acknowledgement owner and adds no copy state, timer,
automatic retry, hidden-textarea fallback or new interaction container.
better-writing's result/recovery guidance and better-accessibility's status and
focus guidance apply to this bounded local action.

| Severity | Location                          | Before                                                     | After                                                        | Why                                                                      |
| -------- | --------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| MEDIUM   | `features/chat/tools/toolMeta.ts` | The injected low-level callback discarded the copy result. | Accepted and refused writes have distinct localized notices. | Users must know whether to paste or recover through the existing reader. |

Verification for this surface (2026-10-21):

- Original clipboard/application baseline passed **2 files / 4 tests**. Bounded edit-only Claude Code CLI supplied twelve scoped files in **134.04 seconds**, without denials or subagents. Primary corrected an invalid test status, used a contract-shaped unknown Tool, restored the previous locale and aligned French/Spanish parameter nouns. Focused typecheck, **3 files / 11 tests** and **1,083 keys across 8 locales** pass. The five denied/absent/composed-outcome regressions fail on the frozen original while six unchanged-behavior cases pass; both candidate sources were restored byte-for-byte. Tests install the real plugin contribution, retain exact whitespace/CJK, use the actual boolean-returning primitive, cover opt-in silence and resolve the locale after installation.
- A private entry wrapper rejects only the first `Clipboard.writeText` with a controlled NotAllowedError; later writes delegate to the actual original API. Its **8 Items** pass the current SDK validator with zero violations. Actual Wails original EN/light14/Orange first copy produced no failure notice. A second copy pasted the exact parameters but produced no success notice. This is controlled API-boundary fault injection; no real OS-denial, permission change, live Tool execution or Runtime write is claimed.
- Actual Wails EN/light/14 px/Flame Orange candidate shows the recovery notice after the refused write while keeping the original Copy action focused. Keyboard expansion reaches Arguments and its named reader; native Select All/Copy then pastes the exact single-record parameters, CJK and double spaces into the retained draft. A confirmed keyboard retry succeeds through the real original API and shows Arguments copied. Native Left-half/200% pointer copy shows a readable acknowledgement and action focus. Actual Size/body wheel retain the draft, Tool content and trajectory query `read`.

## Tool row action context

Single and grouped Tool row actions now reference their existing visible summary.
Extension actions and the workspace opener keep their concise localized names
and Tooltips; their descriptions identify the corresponding act, object and any
status already present in that summary. Expanded results, arguments and error
bodies remain separate reading content rather than button descriptions.

`ToolCard` and `ToolGroupMember` each supply a React `useId` to their summary
and `useToolRowActions`. `AgentActivityDisclosure` accepts an optional `summaryId`
and retains its unconditional generated default for other callers. The same
summary ID names its existing detail region, and a non-disclosable/denied record
still has a static summary. Runtime IDs remain keys and command targets. The
repair adds no duplicate record text and preserves business state, grouping,
layout and navigation owners.

Read-only ZCode `ToolSummaryRow.tsx` keeps kind, object and status together and
accepts an explicit action label; it supports contextual identity rather than
this particular IDREF technique. ChatGPT's actual `tool-activity-disclosure`
module separates summary from inert/hidden expanded content. Flame's established
remembered-rule action association supplies the local IconButton/Tooltip
composition. better-accessibility's relationship guidance and better-writing's
concise action names apply without duplicating the visible record.

## Connection token draft intent

Connection settings now distinguishes a token already set for the current
Runtime address, no current token, and an unapplied token edit. The pending
description explains that Apply is required and a blank input removes the token.
It never projects a secret value or predicts token retention for a draft address.
The existing window-memory hint remains visible; both descriptions and any
token-specific rejection are associated with the password field.

The always-mounted Discard token change action clears only the local token edit,
preserves the URL draft and URL validation feedback, and returns focus to the
token input. It clears a token rejection and invokes no Runtime command.
`ConnectionPane` retains the original Apply, explicit Clear and Reset owners.
The endpoint use case alone trims and validates token input, binds credentials
to the normalized address, and distinguishes omitted from explicit empty input.
All eight locale dictionaries supply the four new descriptions/action labels.

Read-only HeroUI `textfield/with-description.tsx`, `with-error.tsx` and its actual
Description component associate field help and rejection with the input. This
is a semantic reference; Flame retains its shared TextField, FormField,
FormActions, Base UI and StyleX. better-writing's explicit completion/cancellation
guidance and better-accessibility's description/focus guidance apply to this
local edit. Existing typography, semantic surfaces and focus colors remain.

## Hooks scope and source identity

The existing read-only Hooks list now identifies each record's Global or Project
scope and displays its full literal configuration source below the command or
injected text. Identical event/matcher/command entries remain distinguishable,
including an inactive project entry. Paths use the existing small mono step,
quiet foreground and wrapping rule; spaces, CJK and long segments are retained
without truncation, normalization or an implied local file action.

`HookRow` in `features/hooks/ui/HooksPane.tsx` owns this projection. Runtime
`ListHooks` already supplies scope, source and active status, and the existing
query/view model already retains those facts. The repair adds no state, query,
command, trust policy or authoring surface. All eight locale dictionaries supply
the two scope nouns; event, matcher, command/inject and inactive presentation
retain their existing owners.

ZCode's actual `settings/HooksList.tsx` distinguishes installed, plugin and legacy
owners; it supports explicit scope identity, but does not display these exact
source paths. Flame's Runtime contract, not that reference, owns provenance.
better-writing supports direct scope nouns; better-layout/typography support
literal wrapping metadata, and better-accessibility supports exposing row identity
as ordinary readable text rather than relying on a decorative icon.

## Anchored controls and native floating-surface composition

The 2026-10-23 resumed scope starts with the reported **Interface size** and
**Code size** selectors. Font-family pickers use a different Menu composition;
their baseline followed scrolling correctly. The shared-component inspection
traced every in-repository Positioner owner: shared Select, ActionMenu and
Tooltip, application Menu/ContextMenu and Popover/AnchoredPanel, plus the shared
ModalDialog and CatalogPicker compositions. This is a bounded floating-control
repair, not a declaration that every page or component state has passed review.

`packages/ui/src/select.tsx` now sets `alignItemWithTrigger={false}` on Base UI's
Positioner. Base UI 1.9's selected-item overlap mode uses a fixed position and
turns off ancestor-scroll, element-resize and layout-shift anchor tracking.
Settings has its own scrolling viewport, so the trigger could move while the
list stayed behind. Actual Wails reproduced that detached Code size popup,
including a blank overlap, before the change. Ordinary anchored positioning
keeps the existing collision bounds, selected value and focus owners; neither
settings field gets a local event handler, observer, state or offset workaround.
Portable filter controls consume the same repair.

Native 200% acceptance then exposed a second cause in
`packages/ui/src/floating-surface.tsx`: the scrolling list had valid option
rectangles, opacity 1, visible state and contrasting foreground/background, yet
painted only its empty surface. In the visible, focused WKWebView, disabling only
`--floating-backdrop` immediately revealed the same options without moving them.
The final surface applies that existing backdrop-filter token directly to its
face and removes the negative-layer `::before`. The 200% list then paints and
scrolls correctly with the original blur restored. This repairs the common face
used by Select, ActionMenu, Menu, ContextMenu, Popover and Tooltip, while keeping
background, theme/custom-accent ownership, corners, shadow and motion tokens.
ModalDialog retains its separate bounded task and backdrop contracts.

References are read-only. HeroUI's local select/listbox example keeps selected
identity with the control owner; Arc's real `morph-select` implementation bounds
option scrolling and keyboard navigation. Neither supplies Flame's portal
geometry. The installed Base UI Positioner and anchor-positioning implementation
establish the actual tracking contract. better-layout supports a stable trigger
relationship and reachable overflow; better-accessibility supports scoped
keyboard/focus behavior; better-ui supports one compositing owner and restrained
routine feedback. The six better domain skills were loaded; no new palette,
typeface or wording convention was introduced.

| Severity | Domain | Location                                 | Before                                                                                          | After                                                                         | Why                                                                            |
| -------- | ------ | ---------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| HIGH     | Layout | `packages/ui/src/select.tsx:97`          | A size list could stay at its old screen location after the enclosing pane scrolled.            | Use normal anchored positioning with Base UI tracking and collision handling. | A shared popup must stay associated with the control it changes.               |
| HIGH     | UI     | `packages/ui/src/floating-surface.tsx:7` | A negative-layer backdrop inside a scrolling face could hide valid options at native page zoom. | Apply the same backdrop filter directly to the face.                          | Options must remain visibly readable at 200%, including within a bounded list. |

## Immediate popup dismissal and keyboard continuity

Base UI marks a Menu Escape dismissal with `data-instant="dismiss"`. The shared
floating motion now honors that existing signal with `transition-property: none`.
Previously it still ran the host's short exit transition, whose default base is
80 ms. While the retiring portal and focus guards remained mounted, consecutive
native Escape/Tab could leave focus on the document; the following Tab restarted
at Settings Close. Both pointer and pure keyboard menu entry reproduced this.
Stepped dismissal and forward navigation were already correct.

`packages/ui/src/floating-surface.tsx` owns this presentation rule for product and
portable floating surfaces. Base UI still owns open state, focus guards, portal
retirement and final focus. The rule adds no focus callback, delay, handler,
observer or state. Ordinary transitions, other instant reasons, motion tokens,
scrims, layout and explicit caller policies retain their owners. Provider/model
choice, pending feedback and accepted identity remain application/Runtime facts.

The installed Base UI popup attribute contract identifies this as a requested
instant transition, rather than a model-role-specific exception. HeroUI's local
single-selection example keeps checked identity with its selection owner;
better-accessibility's focus-order guidance and better-ui's immediate routine
feedback support completing dismissal before the next keyboard step.

| Severity | Location                                  | Before                                               | After                                                                                                   | Why                                                                                                      |
| -------- | ----------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| MEDIUM   | `packages/ui/src/floating-surface.tsx:25` | Immediate Escape/Tab could restart page focus order. | The existing dismissal signal ends transitions; native forward focus reaches the adjacent role control. | Focus-order continuity (2.4.3) and an immediate dismissal that carries no additional visual information. |

Select has a different primitive contract: Base UI 1.9 exposes its authoritative
`open` state but no instant-dismiss attribute. `SelectPopup` keeps its focus
manager enabled until unmount, and the inherited exit transition delays that
cleanup. On exact main `121bec6d5`, three native Default-motion trials of
Interface size → Escape (35 ms) → Tab (35 ms) left focus on the document; Space
then scrolled the page. Pure keyboard entry reproduced it too. The same three
trials with Motion Off all reached Code size and opened it. The adjacent
font-family Menu already passed with Default motion.

`packages/ui/src/select.tsx` now projects that existing `open` state through the
primitive's `className(state)` contract. Its one StyleX merge keeps the shared
entry styles while open and applies `transition-property: none` while closed.
No second open state, dismissal attribute, event handler, focus callback or delay
was added. Base UI still owns value, guards, unmount and final focus; the hidden
owner's `finalFocus={false}` remains authoritative. Both package and application
StyleX builds use static classes with `dev: false`, so selecting their class name
does not discard a dynamic inline-style value. General floating motion is
unchanged. This fixes appearance sizes, installed filters and nested form choices
through their existing shared Select.

The [Radix Select keyboard model](https://www.radix-ui.com/primitives/docs/components/select#keyboard-interactions)
returns Escape to its trigger. The installed implementation and
[Base UI Popup contract](https://base-ui.com/react/components/select#popup)
establish the actual state and focus owner here. Better-accessibility's focus
order and better-ui's information-free exit guidance support removing the delay
at the Select presentation boundary, without replacing the primitive.

| Severity | Location                        | Before                                                                                      | After                                                                                    | Why                                                                           |
| -------- | ------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| HIGH     | `packages/ui/src/select.tsx:22` | A retiring Select could absorb rapid forward navigation and leave Space scrolling the page. | Dismissal releases focus before the next Tab; entry motion and selected identity remain. | The next keyboard action must continue from the control the user just closed. |

### Shared modal dismissal

Exact main `66e66c6de` exposed the remaining contract difference in the real
sidebar SearchOverlay: an ordinary Escape returned to Search sessions and the
next Tab reached New session, but all **three** consecutive 35 ms Escape/Tab
trials stayed on Search sessions. The same product inputs with Motion Off passed
**three of three**. Base UI 1.9 Dialog keeps its focus manager enabled until the
retiring popup unmounts, and its `data-closed` signal does not use Menu's instant
dismiss attribute. The common 80 ms exit therefore consumed the next action.
CatalogPicker is a Popover, not a Dialog; its existing instant dismissal passed
all three baseline trials. Schedules conditionally unmounts its edit dialog and
also passed all three. Those controls were not defective or redesigned.

`packages/ui/src/floating-surface.tsx` now ends panel and scrim transitions when
Base UI marks a modal `data-closed`. `modalPanel()` merges that modal policy after
the common motion group. One property list and one easing constant own the
unchanged entry declarations. Base UI retains open state, guards, unmount and
return focus; callers retain their initial/final-focus and hidden-owner policies.
SearchOverlay, ModalDialog and transcript LightboxDialog use this same boundary.
Select, Menu, ordinary floating panels and tips keep their existing policies.
No state, timer, event handler, focus callback, observer or dependency was added.

[Radix Dialog's keyboard model](https://www.radix-ui.com/primitives/docs/components/dialog#keyboard-interactions)
and the installed [Base UI Dialog contract](https://base-ui.com/react/components/dialog)
anchor return and modal isolation. Better-accessibility's focus order and
better-ui's information-free exit guidance support making the next action
available immediately. The read-only Arc Dialog implementation uses a different
Radix/Motion presence owner; its lifecycle was not copied. HeroUI's real modal
form and dismissal examples informed the bounded-task comparison.

## Object identity in context menus

Native inspection of exact main `a1ca0566e` found unnamed root menus for Dock
views, Session identity, messages and file entries. These pointer context menus
have no ordinary Menu controlling-button name relationship. Their product owners
already hold the displayed identity, so each root now uses that identity as its
accessible name: view/session titles, message authors and complete file paths.
`SessionRow` reuses its existing trimmed title or localized Untitled session
fallback. `MessageBlock` supplies the same role label used by its visible heading.
`AgentDockTab.title` now states the actual string contract supplied by ChatPanel;
icons and badges keep their separate slots. All real callers were checked before
narrowing the previously unused ReactNode possibility. No second identity state,
generic required-name API, focus handler or translation was added. Nested Restore
keeps the primitive's controlling-item relationship.

The [W3C menu pattern](https://www.w3.org/WAI/ARIA/apg/patterns/menubar/)
recommends an author-provided label or a relationship to the controlling element.
The generic menu role does not universally require an accessible name; this
change makes the current object explicit within Flame's task model. Better
accessibility's meaningful-name and focus-order guidance informed the review.
[Radix Context Menu](https://www.radix-ui.com/primitives/docs/components/context-menu)
and the installed Base UI lifecycle supplied the keyboard comparison. Native
baseline already passed true Shift+F10 entry, arrow navigation, stepped Escape
return and three 35 ms Escape/Tab/Space trials from a focused Dock tab. Its Tab
control reached Browse panels. This batch improves naming and qualifies that
existing focus behavior; it does not claim to repair a ContextMenu focus defect.

## Native workspace zoom and Dock geometry

macOS View commands retain the default menu roles, shortcuts, 5% steps and
Actual Size floor. `desktop/window_zoom_darwin.go` replaces only their callbacks;
`desktop/window_chrome_darwin.go` reads and changes `WKWebView.pageZoom` on the
AppKit main thread. WebKit owns the scale. The exact window's public content
view hierarchy supplies its WebView. Native button coordinates are divided by
that scale before crossing the existing CSS-pixel boundary, and the native
fullscreen style mask supplies measured zeroes. Other platforms keep their
Wails menu implementation. Dependencies and IPC methods are unchanged.

The pinned Wails macOS roles used viewport magnification. Production first
reproduced an outer scroll area and missing sidebar footer after a single Zoom
In. Switching to page zoom exposed a second problem: at 200%, a collapsed
320 px Dock had a -640 px margin, making the 720 px conversation 1040 px wide.
The Dock now collapses its flex basis to zero. While open, the duplicate CSS
width formula also drifted: at 120%, End painted approximately 518 px in a
925 px row and left 407 px for reading instead of the existing 440 px floor.

`ChatPanel.tsx` now projects the observed row through `shellGeometry` once,
using the saved ratio or automatic width. `AgentDockRow` renders that pixel
measure and `DockResizer` consumes the same committed width. Row observation
advances no saved preference. The retired `dockWidth.ts` CSS arithmetic and its
exclusive CSS/TypeScript comparison probes are removed; the independent name
legibility probe remains. The preferred-width constant is now private to its
actual algorithm owner.

Direct pointer and keyboard resizing suspend width transitions. Opening and
closing retain their visibility timing, surfaces and seam. The host structure
stays mounted; installed-view continuity is qualified in the section above.
`ResizeHandle`
retires a live preview when a new committed width arrives, preserving the new
projection after rollback; cancellation without that replacement retains the
captured value and priority. Its accessible value and Dock gesture origin use
the committed pixel width, avoiding a cached intermediate animation frame.

Reference evidence: ZCode's read-only preload zoom code uses native content zoom
and divides macOS control padding by the current factor. The local Apple SDK
and [Apple pageZoom documentation](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/webkit/wkwebview/pagezoom)
distinguish content scaling from viewport magnification; page zoom is available
below the project's macOS 13 floor. Public [Wails menu APIs](https://v3.wails.io/reference/menu/)
provide the callback boundary; the installed beta.29 source establishes the
actual behavior. The better-layout/accessibility review drove viewport reflow,
reachable controls and width semantics. Existing type, color and surface owners
remain the visual basis.

For captured Tool JSON fidelity:

- The baseline model changed `9007199254740993` to `9007199254740992`, converted an overflowing exponent to `null`, collapsed negative zero and dropped earlier duplicate members. The existing result Copy button followed by system paste produced the same incorrect text. The captured `argumentsText` and result strings themselves remained intact.
- `chat/tools/application/toolInspectorModel.ts` now uses the existing `jsonc-parser` formatter's whitespace edits. The parsed object is discarded after JSON syntax qualification; it is never serialized for this view. Expected syntax errors preserve literal text, while unexpected failures propagate. Existing two-space/LF indentation, scalar/plain-text handling, empty-state rules, result-first placement, secondary Arguments and clipboard/selection owners remain. Escapes such as `\u4e2d` now stay visibly escaped, which is intentional source fidelity.
- Claude Code CLI (`claude-opus-5-5`, no subagents or permission denials) supplied the bounded implementation and regressions. The new baseline failed six cases and passed ten. Final targeted CLI checks passed 16 cases and all 47 Tool cases; the primary focused run passed ten files and 53 tests, including ShikiCodeBlock. Tests use literal expected text for unsafe IDs, uint64, precise decimals, signed exponents, negative zero, duplicate members, nested structures and escaped strings. Strict malformed/JSONC text remains literal, existing markup-safety assertions remain, and the composed Copy button receives the same accurate presented result.

For the oversized native editor investigation:

- Actual Wails on macOS 26.5 used English/dark/18 px/custom blue in a 1440 × 900 viewport, with the production editor over isolated canonical reads. The exact generated source remains **1,188,891 characters / 50,002 lines**, including two trailing newlines. Read-only diagnostics checked direct equality, native value/default value, selection, focus and geometry. No Send, live Runtime mutation or installation update occurred.
- The final matrix distinguishes conditions that earlier plain-control results did not cover:

  | Native case                                                                                                               | Result                                                                                                                      |
  | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
  | An already attached plain textarea, including mirrored defaults and a 1,350,078 px autosize constrained by CSS max-height | Readable                                                                                                                    |
  | A new plain textarea populated before insertion, with either live value alone or mirrored default/value                   | Accessibility state unavailable                                                                                             |
  | A new plain textarea populated after insertion, or cleared/restored after insertion with its selection preserved          | Readable                                                                                                                    |
  | The latter plain initialization with autosize, modal geometry/semantics, or a separate wrapping-label probe               | Readable in the individually tested combinations                                                                            |
  | Production editor with a once-per-node controlled-mount normalization, including a temporary native height cap            | Accessibility state unavailable                                                                                             |
  | Captured production dialog DOM/CSS without React or Base UI, with that initialization                                     | Accessibility state unavailable at the product font; readable with only the native font family changed to generic monospace |
  | Captured static dialog with only normal letter spacing                                                                    | Accessibility state unavailable                                                                                             |
  | Original production implementation with only a temporary generic-monospace editor override                                | Accessibility state unavailable                                                                                             |
  | Production editor with both temporary controlled-mount normalization and generic monospace                                | Readable; native end/edit/undo/cancel returned correctly                                                                    |

- Installed React 19.3.0 source initializes textarea content before insertion; its [textarea contract](https://react.dev/reference/react-dom/components/textarea) leaves controlled updates with React. A temporary native-value trace confirmed the candidate's clear/restore happened while connected, retained the complete default and live values, and restored the original zero-position selection. The approved-font production field still failed. Static DOM/CSS reproduction establishes that React state and Base UI lifecycle are not required to reproduce the remaining failure. Separate passing probes exclude autosize, wrapping labels and modal semantics as sufficient causes on their own. The font-family result is an observed native rendering/accessibility interaction, not a diagnosis of a particular font-file defect, performance bottleneck or upstream WebKit revision.
- The combined experiment retained full accessible state through Cmd-Down, a trusted `X` insertion to 1,188,892 characters and native Undo back to the exact original at caret 1,188,891. Escape retired the editor and returned to the original attachment chip. This qualifies that disposable combination only. The product's approved-font oversized editor remains unqualified; no truncation, text-length font switch, extra controlled-value owner, delayed retry or loading phase was introduced. The existing manual sizing owner and CSS min/max contract remain intact.

Because native production acceptance failed, the candidate and its tests were removed rather than delivered as a fix. After restoring the original primitive and removing those tests, the final shared UI `npm run check` passed its retained 58 tests, all build/type/lint/format checks and the independent packed consumer.
- Isolated fixture builds and the production-tagged native overlay compilation passed with the supported macOS 13 floor and existing non-failing duplicate `-lobjc` warning. A fixture asset-root assembly error was corrected before editor acceptance. No production source, dependency, protocol, persisted-data format or portable resource remains changed. The final delivery is documentation only: the prior frontend and supported Wails production gates remain historical, not newly claimed passes. No browser suite, performance benchmark, VoiceOver, OS IME, other-platform GUI or broader font/theme qualification ran. Native accessibility extraction sometimes reached its tool deadline; this does not prove a general application freeze or physical-keyboard failure.

For the native hidden-zoom investigation:

- Actual Wails on macOS 26.5 used production components and the canonical read-only Tool-shell fixture in English/dark/18 px/custom blue, at a 1440-point window. After hidden zoom to 200%, Close and Actual Size returned that same node at 582,997, around line 23555. Its row height and complete content height remained unchanged.
- A separate plain HTML page in the same real Wails host used one fixed-height overflow container and a tall content child, with no React, virtual rows, ResizeObserver or application scroll handlers. A native button established the same reference position. The identical 150% → hide → 200% → show → Actual Size sequence reproduced exactly 777,329 → 582,997. A separate hide/show at unchanged 100% retained 777,329. Temporary read-only diagnostics observed DOM geometry; they did not restore or synthesize scroll state. No live Runtime write, recorded Tool execution or installed-package update occurred.
- This confirms a native hidden-zoom limitation, not passing reading continuity under that condition. Current upstream WebKit source supplies a consistent save/restore-and-zoom explanation; the exact system WebKit revision and an upstream repair were not qualified. For recorded-evidence selection and copy:

- The final complete `VITEST_MAX_WORKERS=2 npm run check` passed 381 files and 2,820 tests, followed by all type, lint, format, unused-code, architecture, style/token, locale, bootstrap, resource, bundle and production React/JSX linkage gates. The one timeout's cause remains unclassified; recurring loopback `ECONNREFUSED` diagnostics were also present on successful runs.
- New regressions exposed whole-workspace Select All, copied reader framing and live-range shrinkage during virtual-window/page reconciliation. Review also exposed editable-descendant copy overriding a retained reader range. Each was reproduced before its repair. The final focused recorded-result run passed 29 tests; the CLI's visual-unit run passed 13 files and 124 tests. Ordinary partial/cross-boundary/collapsed/handled copy, composition and modifiers, literal prose, ANSI, real blank lines, filtering, paging and exact complete-source bytes remain covered. Test-only ANSI-regex lint and incorrect overscan/trailing-newline expectations were corrected without relaxing the contracts.
- Shared UI `npm run check` passed 58 tests and the independently packed UI/icons consumer. Portable build/check passed all ten exact resources after the stable content boundaries; only the Tool-results ESM changed. No production Go, SDK, Runtime or CLI source changed, so their standalone module checks were not rerun. Existing browser row selectors were migrated for the intentional content boundary, but no browser suite or performance benchmark ran.
- The supported `wails3 task build` production build passed. `file` confirmed arm64 and `vtool` confirmed the macOS 13.0 deployment floor. The duplicate `-lobjc` linker warning remains non-failing. There is no dependency, persisted-data or protocol migration.
- Actual Wails acceptance used English/dark/18 px/custom blue in a 1440-point window and Chinese/light/14 px/Flame Orange after system Move & Resize produced a 1122 × 950 CSS viewport. A real pointer drag reduced the light Dock from 364 to 320 px. The final rapid expansion → Page Up → Select All → Copy sequence supplied the complete **1,188,891-character** ANSI-stripped source, including two trailing newlines, in both scenes. Only 29–32 output rows were mounted. The earlier structure had fallen back to partial native copy during that same rapid light sequence; the stable content child resolved it without selection state or retries.
- Native source-result copies contained the presented 40 rows (1,599 characters), 80 rows (3,199) or filtered record 42 (39), excluding the `Line 42` framing that the first candidate actually pasted. The preview's nine presented lines copied 192 characters with a real blank instead of padding spaces. Real mouse selection and system paste preserved exactly one 23-character output line. Full-output system paste produced a 50,002-line Composer text fragment. Find retained native input Select All/Copy, and Escape returned focus to Tool output; subsequent selection, virtual scrolling and complete copy remained correct. Native Tab to paging normally retired the old selection; the app does not recreate it.

It did not execute recorded Tools, mutate a live Runtime, update an existing installation or qualify native resource replacement. Rebuilt sources/resources must still be delivered through the normal installation owner. VoiceOver, OS IME, other platform GUIs and rendered pixel contrast were not qualified. The exploratory hidden-zoom reading change and oversized-paste preview limit below are not passing continuity/preview claims. An unsupported `zh-CN` fixture query was corrected to the registered `zh` locale; it was an acceptance setup error, not a product defect.

For the subsequent native keyboard investigation:

- Actual Wails on macOS 26.5 qualified the unchanged default host in English/light/14 px/Flame Orange. Tab from Approval mode reached the resize handle; Tab entered the selected Dock tab and Shift-Tab returned to the handle. Home committed 320 px, End committed 725 px, Right reduced width by 8 px and Shift-Right by 24 px. Focus remained on the named separator and the semantic focus outline was visible. This is focused keyboard qualification, not a fresh pass of every earlier theme/window scenario.
- Clicking the tab label, by accessibility action or real screenshot-coordinate mouse input, left repeated Shift-Tab on that tab. Read-only event diagnostics showed an unprevented Tab event and no focus transition. Forward Tab to Browse panels, followed by two Shift-Tabs, reached the handle. A disposable bare HTML page with three ordinary buttons and a nested text span reproduced the clicked-label behavior without React, Base UI or Flame's focus handlers; pure keyboard traversal remained reversible there as well.
- A controlled native A/B explicitly enabled Wails' existing `TabFocusesLinks` preference in an external Go overlay. It did not change the clicked-label result; the normal keyboard path also worked with the production default. The result matches the focus-navigation starting-point issue described in [WHATWG HTML #12514](https://github.com/whatwg/html/issues/12514) and its [proposed specification repair](https://github.com/whatwg/html/pull/12843). This is an evidence-backed platform limitation, not a resize-algorithm failure. Revisit it when the underlying WebKit behavior changes; do not add a competing application Tab order or change users' OS settings.
- Production source, dependencies and generated resources were unchanged. The isolated fixture build and native overlay compilation passed; the duplicate `-lobjc` warning remained non-failing. Only this documentation changed, so verification used its formatter and `git diff --check`; prior full gates remain historical evidence rather than newly run checks. No browser suite, performance benchmark, OS IME or VoiceOver check ran. No bounded implementation was delegated because the investigation justified no production change.
