A repo-wide quality pass (recorded in commits, not duplicated here) cleared the
remaining non-visual bad smells across the web UI, `packages/ui` and the plugin
components: dead exports and a whole dead MCP server-reconnect chain, three
one-fact owners re-centred (composer sendable-content, usagereports period labels,
the inspection return-dock rule), and two logic fixes (per-asset brand-download
spinner; the diff panel degrades an unknown comparison mode instead of throwing).
A reference-alignment review confirmed the conversation reading model already
matches ChatGPT/Codex structurally — reading axis, wide-content breakout, reasoning
height cap and "Thought for X" collapse, tool-group summarization, meaningful-result
promotion, and the opacity-ladder hierarchy.

A further structural pass (recorded in commits) continued the one-fact/one-owner
and broken-intent cleanup, grounded in source audits of the type scale, interaction
states, settings/layout, chat, composer and sidebar surfaces against the ChatGPT/
Codex references. It regularised the display type tier to an 18/20/24 heading scale
(dropping the off-grid 17px step and unifying the tier's tracking and leading);
wired the missing and mismatched state-transition durations so hover washes, the
choice-list row, the shared chevron and the mermaid toolbar fade instead of
snapping; upgraded selected rows to the selected-hover ink; and re-centred several
owners — the settings list-row lead gap, the jump-to-bottom float dock, the
question-card footer, the nested-row indent (which fixed a real 2px show-more
misalignment), and the three hand-rolled sidebar status dots (now one StatusDot with
a ring variant). Composer and rail corner radii now name the radius token, and dead
letter-spacing vars plus an orphaned spacing rung were pruned. One correctness fix
landed: the composer now refuses a fresh turn while the root run is waiting, so the
keyboard Enter path enforces the same predicate the disabled send button already
showed. One misstep was self-corrected: a density/reading rhythm rewrite to
calc(var(--spacing)*N) was reverted because those custom properties are owned by the
TypeScript density ladder (density-scaled) and fixed reading edges that the
stylesheet only mirrors — a stylesheet-mirror test caught it. A renderHook
regression for the waiting-turn fix is deferred to the consolidated testing pass
(the pure admission rule is already covered).

A dedicated accessibility/keyboard pass (code-verifiable defects only, recorded in
commits) followed. It converted the theme, font and language pickers from
DropdownMenu.Item to the RadioGroup/RadioItem/RadioItemIndicator pattern the model-
role picker already used, so the selected option now carries menuitemradio +
aria-checked instead of a decorative check; routed the project row's "working
directory missing" state into the row's own aria-label (the state had been passed to
an Icon that drops aria-label and forces aria-hidden); and gave the plugin error
disclosure the aria-expanded every other disclosure has. The @flame/ui primitive
layer and the dialog/combobox/live-region wiring were verified clean. One low-
severity gap is left: the session row's favorite star is visual-only in the row's
accessible name — folding it in needs a new "favorited" state string across eight
locales and the state is already reachable from the row menu, so it is deferred
rather than guessed.

A cross-feature consistency pass (the orthogonal view to the per-surface audits)
confirmed empty-state, loading-skeleton, pending-button and master-detail return
patterns are already centrally owned, and fixed the two divergences it found: the
provider/model-role/MCP forms rendered the shared useAsyncFeedback error through
three mutually-inconsistent hand-rolled alerts instead of SystemMessage (now all on
SystemMessage), and the MCP server-toggle and approval mode-change re-implemented
the useCommandAction toast-on-failure flow by hand — the server toggle without its
in-flight guard — while sibling rows in the same features already used the helper
(now both routed through it).

Deeper verification lenses (state ownership, hooks/effects, layout-correctness,
i18n usage, untrusted-content security, and motion / reduced-motion) came back
clean except for the turn-rail session-switch rebind and the banner-card grid
hardening recorded above. Reduced motion in particular is sound across three
coordinated layers: a global CSS `!important` catch-all that forces every
animation/transition to 0s and a single iteration under both the OS
`prefers-reduced-motion` query and the in-app `data-motion="off"` toggle, the
`--motion-scale` token wrapping every `--dur-*`/`--animate-*` duration, and a
`MotionConfig reducedMotion="user"` plus `motionScale()` gate on the motion/react
layer.
A follow-up repo-wide motion/grid sweep confirmed no `.tsx` still carries a raw
`var(--dur|ease|animate-*)` string or a hard-coded framer `duration:` (all route
through the `motion.*` tokens / `lib/motion` variants), and the sidebar carries no
off-grid literal. It found and fixed one residual: the truncate fade-mask edge was
the literal `16px` spelled three times — the trailing fade on `.truncate-fade` and
the leading fade reached by the `agent-overflow-lead` marquee keyframe — which must
read as one fade yet could drift. `.truncate-fade` now owns it as
`--truncate-fade-width` on the 4px grid (output-identical) and every mask reads that
one owner, so the marquee's leading and trailing fades move together by
construction. The `AgentOverflowLabel` marquee duration stays distance-proportional
(`distance / (fontSize * 2)`, constant speed) and still multiplies `--motion-scale`,
so reduced motion zeroes it.
A dedicated subagent re-read the whole message/transcript rendering surface
(ChatStream, MessageStream, MessageTurn, BlockRenderer, every card, the markdown
subtree, ImagePreviewGallery, SubagentsPanel, ChatPanel) and it came back clean apart
from one correctness fix and two dead-style removals, now shipped: `NarrativeWave`
keyed its sub-units by array index while its sibling `renderMessageBlocks` keys the
same `MessageRenderUnit[]` by content identity (`renderUnitKey`); since a wave child
can be a stateful `ReasoningBlock`, a membership/order shift between streaming renders
would have stranded an expanded/elapsed state on the wrong block, so the message id is
now threaded through `renderUnit` into the wave and both renderers key identically.
The unreferenced `messageStyles.statusWord` and `RunErrorBanner` `reb.plain` style
keys were deleted. The only off-grid raw-px spacing left in `apps/web/src` —
`ProjectSelector` `marginBottom: -18px` / `paddingBottom: 27px` — stays as a
deliberate optical tuck (it tucks the project tray behind the composer with a ~9px
lip); its values are not 4px-grid multiples, so forcing them onto the grid or into
`space.*` would change the look and needs a native eye, not a blind rewrite.
A second subagent then independently read the navigation / chrome / shared-primitive
surfaces (sidebar, navigation, `ui/agent`) and they came back clean apart from one
token-grid bypass, now fixed: the `AgentStatusPill` floor was a raw `22px` min-height
(between `space.s5` and `s6`, tracking no token) that would not scale with a density
or type-scale change; it is now the `5.5` half-step of the 4px grid
(`calc(var(--spacing) * 5.5)`, output-identical). The remaining `ui/atoms/` primitives
(button, confirm-dialog, data-view, data-error, system-message, skeleton, chip,
chevron, collapsible, catalog-picker, …) were then swept directly: the fixed panel
dimensions there (`catalog-picker` 300/400/280px, `chip` 220px max-width) are
legitimate large layout constants, and `skeleton`'s `key={i}` is correct for a
fixed-count, identity-less, stateless placeholder list — no defects.
A third subagent read the composer input + suggestion system in full (`Composer`,
`useComposerInputController`, the file-mention / slash / suggestion application state,
`TextArea`/`OptionRow`/`Popover`): IME composition commit (229-keyCode + grace window),
content-identity list keys, suggestion-index clamping, caret-insertion math, the
`aria-controls`/`aria-activedescendant` wiring, token usage and dead code all verified
clean. The one low-severity item — the composer `TextArea` sets `aria-controls` +
`aria-activedescendant` but no `aria-expanded`/`role="combobox"` — is reviewed and kept
as-is: this is the industry inline-completion pattern for a _multiline_ autocomplete
(GitHub/Slack @-mention), where `role="combobox"` would make AT mis-announce the
multi-line editor as a single-line edit and a bare `aria-expanded` is out of spec on
`role="textbox"` (and would trip the a11y lint). The popup already signals presence:
`SuggestionPopup` renders `role="listbox"` for options and `role="status"` (aria-live)
for the loading/empty branch, and the `aria-activedescendant` change on open is itself
announced — so adding the combobox role would regress AT, not help.
Of note, a defensive XSS audit traced every dangerouslySetInnerHTML and URL sink:
mermaid SVG (beautiful-mermaid escapes all text/attrs), shiki (escaped code), the
markdown raw-HTML path (remarkLiteralUnknownHtml downgrades every non-allowlisted,
attribute-free inline tag to text before rehype-raw, with markdownUrlTransform
dropping javascript:/data: schemes and external links carrying
rel="noopener noreferrer"), image src (data:image/* allowlist), and KaTeX
(trust:false) are all safe — no exploitable injection. Optional defense-in-depth,
not implemented because it risks the rich katex/shiki/mermaid render and closes no
live path: add rehype-sanitize as a redundant allowlist layer after rehype-raw and
make allowElement an allowlist rather than a denylist.

The genuinely open items are now design and native-acceptance decisions, not
defects; each needs a Wails look and an explicit choice before implementing:

- **Destructive-confirmation tiering — resolved (confirm persisted removals).**
  Session delete and goal replace routed through ConfirmDialog, but MCP server delete
  and API-key clear removed persisted user state on a single click. Resolved by
  levelling up, not down: removing a persisted user-configured entity or secret is a
  genuine data-integrity risk (a cleared key may exist nowhere else; a deleted server
  drops its whole config), so both now open a destructive `ConfirmDialog` matching
  session/goal, with a title/body added across all eight locales and the existing
  action label reused as the confirm. The explicit tier is: reversible toggles act
  immediately; irreversible removal of persisted user state confirms. (`ServerForm`
  delete-flow tests click through the dialog, scoping to the `alertdialog` since its
  confirm shares the "Delete" label.)

- **Context Dock vs ZCode (reviewed; file-type icon landed).** The dock's two
  master→detail panels (file, subagents) already match or exceed ZCode on identity
  ownership, mount/scroll retention, return semantics, and the centralised
  empty/loading/error contract (`DataView`). One clear gap was fixed: the file detail
  header now shows its file-type icon (reusing the already-computed `fileIconName`,
  which had only fed the unsupported empty state) so the object's kind reads at a
  glance. The two in-view master→detail back controls now share the `‹` chevron
  (subagent's `list` glyph was the outlier), keeping the `‹` in-view-back vs `←`
  dock-return convention; their aria-labels still differ in framing (file "Back to
  files" vs subagent "All subagents"), left as-is since both name an accurate
  destination. **Dock column gutter unified (fixed):** the tab strip aligned its labels
  to the wide reading gutter (20px) while the view content header sits on the compact
  gutter (16px), so a tab and the identity it labels were 4px out of vertical line; the
  strip now references `--layout-compact-gutter` for its start padding (label x = strip
  start + tab inset = 16px), so tab and content read as one column. **Tab size model
  (reviewed — keep):** tabs are content-width (`flex-shrink:0`, 160px label cap) with a
  horizontal-scroll strip and fade masks, matching ZCode/ChatGPT/browser tabs and
  preserving label readability; equal-width-shrink was not adopted. **Strip separation
  (reviewed — keep):** the tabstrip tint band is the softer, modern grouping and is kept
  over a hard divider. Remaining dock items are now resolved: **`SubagentsPanel` chrome gutter aligned
  (fixed)** — its header and link list hand-rolled the wide reading gutter (20px) while
  the sibling file dock panel's header sits on the compact gutter (16px), so the two
  dock panels opened the same column at different left edges; the header and list are
  chrome/navigation (not reading content), so they now use the compact gutter the file
  panel and tab strip share, while the subagent transcript body keeps its own reading
  column. **Two-level header (reviewed — intended):** the dock tab strip is suppressed
  in the promoted `presentedMain` path (`DockHeader` renders only when
  `!presentedMain`), so a tab-selector + object-identity stack appears only in normal
  dock mode, which is the deliberate hierarchy, not a redundancy. **`WorkspaceViewBody`
  default scroll (reviewed — sound):** it is the uniform view host (region, focus,
  `PluginBoundary`, `Suspense`) and its `overflowY:auto` is a default-scroll safety net
  for views that do not self-scroll; for `WorkspaceViewLayout`-based views the
  height-constrained inner `ScrollArea` owns the scroll and the outer never fires (inert,
  no double scrollbar). Removing the outer scroll would require proving every plugin view
  self-scrolls across both render paths, which is not safely knowable, so the uniform
  default-scroll host is kept (KISS + correctness) rather than churned.
  **Reference comparison against ZCode (brief-mandated method):** a dedicated subagent
  read ZCode's side-panel system against Flame's dock on every task-model axis and
  found Flame matches or exceeds it — ZCode uses a split-pane tree (no tab bar, no
  `role="tab"`/`aria-current`, no keyboard tab-switch/close), whereas Flame's dock owns
  roving-arrow tab focus, `Delete`/middle-click/menu close with post-close focus
  settlement, `Escape → returnToSource` cross-view keyboard return, focus-return to the
  originating tree item / run link (`FileWorkspace.tsx:72-87`, `SubagentsPanel.tsx:99-128`;
  ZCode returns focus to neither), `DataView`/`SkeletonList` skeletons, and
  session-persisted open tabs + active view + file path/line + expanded dirs
  (`contextDockStore`, restored cold-start) where ZCode's layout only rehydrates on reload
  and its scroll memory is in-process. The lone implementable delta surfaced —
  `FileWorkspace.tsx:93` unmounts `FilePreview` on return, losing deep scroll on reopening
  the _same_ file where ZCode keeps a per-path scrollTop — is **declined after reading the
  code:** `FilePreview` is a _windowed_ reader whose loaded line window and rendered/source
  mode are state keyed by the `viewer` intent object (`:137-141, 164-168`), so each reopen
  (a new viewer object) deliberately resets the window to `initialWindow(targetLine)`.
  A preserved `scrollTop` is relative to a dynamically-loaded window, so restoring it across
  that intent reset would not line up with the same content — scroll preservation fights the
  windowed-reader design, not just YAGNI. Reopen already anchors to the persisted
  `targetLine` and the file list keeps its own scroll/selection via `<Activity>`. Revisit
  trigger: a user report of losing scroll on file reopen, which would mean rethinking the
  windowed reader toward a stable-document model first. A matching both-panels Esc/Backspace "return to
  this panel's list" (distinct from the conversation-return) is a shared gap in Flame and
  ZCode alike, not a ZCode advantage; if added later, do it symmetrically in both panels.
- **Wide-content boundaries and native math input (qualified within scope).** User/nested boundaries,
  left-anchored outer growth, the shared subagent cap/gutter and exercised
  full-pane/Dock/200% return paths are now qualified above; do not repeat them as
  pending work. A visibly present turn rail was not qualified. The native math
  follow-up now records text-caret navigation, visible final tag and exact draft
  return. Physical horizontal wheel delivery and broader typography/zoom remain
  outside that qualification; do not infer a component defect from offset alone.
- **Standalone ToolCard open-state (reviewed — keep).** A standalone card keeps its
  promoted write/exec result visible via a manual toggle ("meaningful results stay"),
  while the grouped `ToolGroup` auto-collapses on completion. This is a justified
  per-context difference (one promoted result stays legible; a wave of grouped tools
  collapses to cut clutter), not drift — SRP across the two owners — so it is kept
  rather than forced to one behavior.
- **Light-theme text ladder collapse (muted ≈ faint) — fixed, native acceptance
  pending.** A color-semantics audit found the role usage consistent, but one real
  value defect: in the light theme `--color-text-muted` (#656565) and `--color-text-faint`
  (#696969) differed by ~4/255, so the two-tier metadata hierarchy (muted label over
  faint detail) that reads clearly in dark was lost in light across
  `activity-disclosure`, `context-dock`, `QuestionCard`, `kbd`, `text-button` and
  catalog rows. Root cause is the palette ladder: `kit/scale.ts` `NEUTRAL_INK` derives
  muted from step 11 and faint from step 10, and the light neutral scale compressed
  those adjacent ink shares (62 vs 51) while the AA bump then pulled both to the same
  dim floor — on the warm custom light canvas muted and faint came out byte-identical.
  It is AA-constrained: `palette.test.ts` requires every text step, faint included, to
  clear WCAG AA (4.5:1) against all planes, and light faint already sits at that floor,
  so it cannot be lightened. The shipped fix re-spaces the ladder _inside_ the floor by
  darkening the muted ink anchor (step 11) from 62 to 74, measured against the real
  computed values: built-in light now steps evenly (L 0.22 / 0.31 / 0.42 / 0.52) and
  custom light muted/faint separate, faint keeps its floor value, and the dark ladder
  is untouched. `soft` tracks muted through its 55% anchor, so the `:root` mirror moved
  with it (`stylesheetMirror.test.ts` and the 111 `palette.test.ts` AA cases pass).
  Confirm the muted weight across both light canvases in Wails.
- **Reasoning while streaming.** Today a quiet one-line glimpse; ChatGPT shows the
  full capped auto-following panel inline, but the newer ZCode reference explicitly
  reverted from that panel back to a default-collapsed glimpse (an expanded
  streaming reasoning panel keeps crowding tools and body). Flame already matches
  ZCode, and `ReasoningBlock.test.tsx` locks "starts shut while it thinks" as a
  deliberate choice — keep the glimpse.
- **Reasoning dimness (implemented and natively accepted; alpha confirmed optimal).**
  Both references run reasoning dimmer than Flame did (ChatGPT title ≈30% / body
  ≈50–60%; ZCode a uniform base-text ≈40% alpha), and Flame was the brightest of the
  three. A reasoning-only `--color-text-reasoning` token
  (`apps/web/src/styles/globals.css`) now carries the body (`messageStyles.quote`),
  glimpse (`ReasoningBlock` `glimpseLine`) and the disclosure label/detail via an
  `AgentActivityDisclosure` `ink="dim"` variant (tools keep the default ink). The two
  theme alphas are **not** the same number because they are matched on _effective_
  composited contrast, not on alpha: light `var(--color-text) 58%` and dark `45%` both
  land at ~4.0:1 over the canvas (measured: light 58% → 4.11, dark 45% → 4.04), a
  deliberate "quieter than body" tier below AA that both references also run. Literally
  adopting the ZCode 40% figure in light was measured to crash the composite to 2.46:1
  (unreadable), so the reference alpha does not transfer across canvas luminance; the
  current contrast-matched pair is the tuned result and was already accepted in Wails.
  Keep it.
- **Panel-header / two-level return.** The Dock view-header variants and the
  Dock-return (`←`) vs in-view back (`‹`/`list`) affordances could unify into one
  identity+actions+return contract or breadcrumb; this is a focus-and-visual
  redesign, not a cleanup. The `data-chrome-focus` disagreement is resolved: the
  dock view-header back (`FileWorkspace` chevron) now suppresses the outline ring
  like every other chrome navigation control (window-header return/close, sidebar
  rows, menu items), so the two return affordances indicate keyboard focus the same
  way. The icons intentionally differ (`‹` in-view back vs `←` dock-return), so they
  are not duplicate markup to merge; the shared `ReturnButton` already owns the
  arrow/dock-return affordance. The remaining header unification (one
  identity+actions+return contract or breadcrumb) is still a design/native decision.
- **Shared primitives for plugin-component duplication (prioritised backlog).** An
  audit of every `plugins/*/component/**` UI (which may import only `@flame/ui`,
  `@flame/component-sdk` and the wire client — `@flame/component-sdk` is consumed from
  source, so new exports there reach plugins directly) found three real cross-plugin
  duplications, all best fixed by deliberate shared owners plus a plugin ESM rebuild
  and host render verification, not blind partial extraction:
  1. _Master/detail scroll+focus restore_ — the byte-identical `scrollPositions`
     helper and `ScrollPosition` type now have one owner in `@flame/component-sdk`
     (the three inline copies in diff/usagereports/skills are removed). The remaining
     duplication is the orchestration around it — the `pendingFocus` ref and the
     scroll-to-0 / restore-scroll / focus-opener `useLayoutEffect` — which should
     become a `useMasterDetailFocus` hook in `@flame/component-sdk`; it varies per
     plugin (saved-return shape, view literals), so it needs deliberate design and
     host render verification.
  2. _Reconcilable-command state + UI_ — the submit→pending→uncertain→reconcile/retire
     protocol and its status `<section>` are copied across `skills`, `schedules`,
     `memory`, `usagereports`, and (slim) `trajectory`; the `Command` type and
     `reconcile()` are byte-identical, but `observe()`/`submit()` genuinely vary per
     plugin — different success/error copy, different revision-conflict side effects,
     and different state to clear (skills' review, schedules' edit/delete/taskOpener).
     So the owner is a `useReconcilableCommand` hook in `@flame/component-sdk` that
     keeps the pending/uncertain/reconcile machinery and takes the per-plugin
     success/error handling as callbacks (plus an `UnconfirmedCommand` status component
     in `@flame/ui`). The byte-identical `failure()` helper is already extracted
     (`@flame/component-sdk` `failureText`); the remaining duplication is the ~60-line
     stateful body, so the hook is the real fix — a stateful refactor across five
     sandboxed UIs that needs host render verification, not a blind pure extraction
     like the scrollPositions helper above.
  3. _Status/alert text primitive_ — some plugins use the `@flame/ui` `Metadata` muted
     status, others a raw `<p role="status|alert">`; standardise on `Metadata` or add
     a `StatusText` primitive. Also minor: a duplicated `Intl.NumberFormat("en-US")`
     (`usage`/`usagereports`) and a set of unreferenced DOM `id`s that read vestigial
     but may be host deep-link anchors (verify against the sandbox shell before
     removing). Token/alpha and accessibility are otherwise clean across the plugins.
- **Settings-list primitive consistency.** The loaded-plugins list (`PluginsPane`)
  hand-rolls its record rows and a manual expand/collapse where its sibling
  `PackageManager.PackageRow` composes `ListRow` + `RecordHeading` + `Disclosure`;
  `ProviderRow` similarly hand-rolls its header grid. Adopting the shared primitives
  would unify them, but the plugin list's compact error-count-as-toggle and the
  provider row's ≤440px masked-key reflow are deliberate layouts — unifying is a UX
  decision plus a native look, not a blind swap.

- **Plugin render-crash fallback → SystemMessage (implemented; native acceptance
  pending).** The `PluginBoundary` fallback used to hand-roll a negative-toned inline
  card (`overlays.css` `.plugin-boundary-error`: own border, wash, radius, tone) holding
  the `plugins.renderFailed` Trans, a `<code>`, a retry `TextButton` and `FailureDetails`,
  where the consistency pass had already routed form and config alerts onto
  `SystemMessage`. It now renders through `SystemMessage` (`variant="error"`,
  `shape="form"`, retry in the `action` slot); the plugin name, the error `<code>` and
  the `FailureDetails` disclosure are the banner's children, `.plugin-boundary-error`
  keeps only the stacking column, and `.plugin-boundary-actions` is deleted. The
  boundary's `role="alert"`, code tag and retry contract are unchanged
  (`PluginBoundary.test.tsx` 4/4). The crash banner now carries the shared error icon
  and tone instead of its bespoke border — confirm the in-pane look in Wails.

- **Streaming-text animation timings.** The markdown word-reveal (`overlays.css`
  `.fade-in`, `700ms ease-in-out`) and the stream caret (`.type-caret`, `1050ms`
  blink) are the only animations with bespoke millisecond bases rather than the
  `--dur-*` ladder (they do multiply `--motion-scale`, so reduced motion still zeroes
  them). These are content-reveal cadences, not UI-chrome transitions, so they may be
  deliberately off-ladder; mapping them onto `--dur-slow`/`--ease-state` would change
  the actual timing and reveal feel, which needs a Wails look before adopting.

## Dock plugin layout and the task mental model

The user judged the dock plugin views (naming diff and schedules) as less reasonable
than ChatGPT/ZCode and asked for a systematic layout pass referencing them. Grounding:
the dock is a narrow column (`shellGeometry` 320–480px), so competing control chrome
hurts most there; the Ace Design references (`acedesign.io` Alpaca and Marketer, studied
from the cached mockups) show the target composition — a breadcrumb or single object-
identity header, section labels, label-value rows, right-aligned muted metadata, one
purposeful set of actions, and generous whitespace ("let the layout explain"). A
subagent audited every dock plugin against that and the task model; the ranked fixes
shipped so far (each a `plugins/*/component` source edit plus its rebuilt ESM resource;
`cd plugins && npm run build && npm run check` verifies them; native host acceptance of
each dock view is still pending):

- **schedules** — Run now was buried in the "Schedule details" disclosure while
  Edit/Enable/Delete sat in the row action menu, so the primary "what's next" was the
  hardest to reach; Run now is now the first action-menu item and the disclosure is
  read-only facts. The card led with a raw `Cron` code line as identity and repeated
  Next firing; the human-readable Next firing is now the primary line, the cron
  expression moved into the facts, and the duplicate was dropped. The restating panel
  description is gone. The saved-schedules group also moved from the `cards` variant to
  the `list` variant: at the 320–480px dock width the `minmax(20rem, 1fr)` cards grid
  always collapsed to one column, so per-card borders were chrome with no multi-column
  benefit; one divided-row surface matches the ZCode/Codex schedule lists.
- **diff** — the file identity was split between the sticky `DiffFileHeader` (basename)
  and a repeated full-path `Metadata` at the top of the body; `DiffFileHeader` (in
  `@flame/ui`, consumed only by this plugin; `apps/web` uses the unchanged
  `DiffFileList`) now shows name + dimmed directory once and the body line is gone.
  Restating panel description removed. The overview Comparison (Uncommitted/Branch)
  selector is now a files-list control: it renders only in the files-list view, so
  opening a file drops a sticky bar (three → two in the narrow dock) and leads with the
  file navigation + identity, matching ZCode/Codex where scope is list-level (the user
  authorised this breaking change). Refresh stays reachable from the file view (moved
  into the file-navigation row). The comparison-failure contract — retain the last-good
  read, show the stale notice, recover on Refresh — is now retested from the files list
  in `visual/diffPresentation.test.tsx`. (An earlier attempt that hid the whole
  comparison bar including Refresh broke the file-view Refresh cases; keeping Refresh in
  the file-nav row is the fix.)
- **usage** — merging the period Toolbar and the search/group FilterBar into one bar
  was tried and reverted: `visual/usageScopePresentation.test.tsx` asserts the report
  range (period) precedes the local filters, and FilterBar leads with search, which
  inverted that order. The scope-bar-then-filter-bar split is the intended "set the
  range, then filter within it" order and is kept.
- **usagereports, skills** — the detail view stacked a navigation Toolbar (Back +
  Refresh) over a second Toolbar holding only the title; collapsed into one row (Back +
  title + skills' scope badge as content, Refresh as the trailing action).
- **memory** — the editor restated the scope a third time (already in the always-visible
  Segmented and the header); dropped, leaving only the record id on an update. The
  deeper item — the edit `RecordForm` sits above the still-rendered list rather than a
  list→detail switch — is NOT a plain list→detail fix: `current` stays truthy during an
  edit so the catalog renders below the editor on purpose, and
  `visual/memoryDrafts.test.tsx` ("keeps editor and record feedback apart") locks in
  that you can Approve/act on other rows while editing one. Hiding the list would break
  that coexistence; the real improvement is in-place row editing (editor replacing the
  edited row), a larger refactor to weigh against the coexistence contract and verify
  natively — deferred, not a blind swap.
- A second deep audit covered the plugins the first pass only skimmed (trajectory,
  tool-results, diagnostics, file-renderers, icon-gallery). Two real defects were fixed:
  `file-renderers` re-ran the `startLine !== 1 || truncated` truncation check and
  returned invented "use the source view" copy, but its host `RecordedFileComponent`
  already applies that predicate and returns the real fallback before mounting — a dead,
  unreachable branch duplicating a host-owned decision, now removed; and `diagnostics`
  set a metric row's description to `row.description || row.kind` while the status Badge
  already owns `kind`, so an empty-description metric printed the kind twice (now falls
  back to no description). `tool-results` and `icon-gallery` are clean (the `key={index}`
  in tool-results is correct for its deliberately-stable stateless row container; the
  icon-gallery `title` is the article's accessible name, pinned by a test). `trajectory`
  is clean apart from expanded rows re-printing a couple of summary values — a deliberate
  preview-in-summary / labelled-in-detail idiom, left as-is. `usage`'s input/output token
  overlap and the `diagnostics` "Attributes" `PanelSection` heading nesting are low-value
  items left for a later pass.
- The settings "resource library" panes (`PackageManager`, `PluginsPane`,
  `ProviderSelections`, `PendingCommands`, `InstalledComponent`/`ComponentCompositions`)
  were then audited for the same browse→read→return lens. One real defect was fixed:
  `ProviderSelections` wrapped a `DataView` (loading/empty/error + a count) around
  `providers.data` (the recorded selections), but the browsable content is the role list,
  whose roles derive from installation contributions independently of `providers.data` —
  so with roles present but no recorded selection the "No provider has been selected"
  empty card rendered above a working role list, and the skeleton/error showed while the
  roles rendered anyway. The role list is now the state host (shown whenever there are
  roles; `DataView` owns empty/loading/error only when there are none), and the redundant
  count line (restating each row's selected/unselected) is gone. Also minor: the
  package-manager release-manifest `CodeBlock` reused the enclosing "Release details"
  disclosure's label as its region name; it now has its own `packages.manifest` name.
  `PendingCommands`, the index keys (stateless/non-reorderable), the edit-dialog identity
  repeat (intentional, pinned), and the `InstalledComponent`/`ComponentCompositions`
  mount pipeline (deliberate lightweight error-boundary fallbacks, pinned) were verified
  clean.
- The MCP / hooks / approvals / connection settings panes + forms were then audited.
  Two fixes: the MCP server row's transport badge rendered the raw `streamableHttp` enum
  while the edit form localizes the same value to "Streamable HTTP" — the badge now uses
  the `mcp.transport.*` label (one vocabulary); and `ApprovalsPane` early-returned a
  full-pane `DataError` on an approval-mode read failure, unmounting the independent
  `RulesRow` (its own `DataView`) — the mode error now lives in the mode row's slot so the
  rules list survives a mode-only failure. This "error hosted on the wrong collection /
  unmounting independent content" shape appeared twice (here and `ProviderSelections`);
  the remaining `DataError` sites are correct — `ToolControls` scopes three independent
  errors by design, and every other pane routes error through a `DataView` wrapping its
  own list. Verified intentional (pinned by tests, not changed): `HooksPane` scope text,
  `ModeRow` aria+visible label pattern, `ConnectionPane`'s separate token state/hint
  paragraphs and four-op auxiliary stack, and `ServerForm`'s disabled immutable-name
  field (identity echo justified by the create/edit-parallel, name-is-immutable
  affordance).
- The shell/workbench chrome (session identity, run status pill, banners, project
  selector, window header, floating composer) was audited last and came back clean apart
  from one "let the layout explain" item, now fixed: the project picker trigger showed a
  visible "Choose project" label, repeated it as the `title` tooltip ("Choose a project
  to run this chat"), and the dropdown opened with a "Select your project" heading over
  the project list — three labels for one control. The tooltip and heading (and their
  now-unused i18n keys + heading style/imports) are gone; the button names itself and the
  list + "New project" name the menu. Everything else verified clean: `SessionIdentity`
  vs the OS `document.title` are separate legitimate projections (not competing owners);
  `RunStatusPill`/`RunAnnouncer` share one run-status owner; `RunErrorBanner`'s faint
  `error.code` is a deliberate, test-pinned reference; and `FloatingComposer` /
  `CwdMissingBanner` route through the shared `SystemMessage`/`EmptyState` owners.
- The shared `@flame/ui` interactive primitives (`select`, `segmented`, `disclosure`,
  `modal-dialog`, `floating-surface`, `switch`/`checkbox`/`slider`, `action-menu`,
  `choice-list`, and the `menu`/`popover`/`confirm-dialog`/`catalog-picker`/`search-overlay`
  wrappers) were audited on the Radix interaction lens (keyboard, focus enter/restore,
  trigger↔content ARIA, modal boundaries). They came back clean — the trigger/content
  relationships, `aria-expanded`/`aria-controls`/`aria-activedescendant`, hidden-owner
  `finalFocus`, and pending-but-focusable semantics are correctly delegated to Base UI or
  hand-wired and are locked by `components.test`/`popup-visibility.test`/`stateAttributes`.
  One latent trap was hardened: `ModalDialog` only rendered `auxiliaryAction` inside the
  `footer && …` branch, so a dialog passing only `auxiliaryAction` would silently drop it;
  the footer region now renders when either is present (no change for the current
  consumers, which all pass a footer). Left as intentional/harmless: `segmented`'s
  `role="tab"` value-selectors (consistent, test-pinned, no dangling `aria-controls`) and
  `action-menu`'s redundant menu-level `disabled` in the per-item condition (unreachable
  while the trigger is disabled).
- The `@flame/ui` display primitives (`records`, `data-table`, `code-block`, `callout`,
  `badge`, `empty-state`, `item-highlight`) and the `tool-results` plugin renderer were
  swept last. The only candidate was hardcoded-English strings (`callout`'s
  Note/Tip/Important/Warning/Caution titles; `tool-results`' Find/Copy/row-count copy).
  Verdict: not a smell — both layers are English-by-design. `@flame/ui` has zero i18n
  (no `useT`/`i18n`/translation anywhere in the package; strings are English defaults the
  consumer overrides via props), and the callout titles mirror the GFM `[!NOTE]` marker
  keyword the author wrote, matching GitHub's own alert rendering. The plugin sandbox is
  the same: `@flame/component-sdk` exports no i18n channel, so every dock plugin
  (schedules, diff, usage, memory, …) is English by construction. Localizing either layer
  is a feature addition (new override props / a sandbox i18n channel + per-plugin
  catalogs), not a bad-smell fix, and is out of scope for this pass. On the mental-model
  axis `tool-results` is clean: it owns only the result presentation (CodeFrame + line
  numbers + find + paging), while the enclosing tool-result record header owns "where am
  I / which object" — the result-vs-detail split is correct. This closes the systematic
  dock-plugin + shared-primitive sweep; all substantive dock views (schedules, diff,
  usage, usagereports, skills, memory, diagnostics, trajectory, tool-results, file-
  renderers) now conform to the task model, and the one remaining open item is native
  host acceptance of the shipped visual changes, which only the user can perform.
- A final cross-plugin pass compared the eight list/detail views on one axis — do two
  plugins solve the _same_ chrome problem differently without cause. Consistent already:
  every list uses `PanelHeader`; per-object identity (diff/memory/trajectory) always lands
  in `PanelHeader context`; pagination is uniformly `PanelFooter` + a pages `Toolbar`. Two
  genuine divergences were fixed. (1) **Refresh vocabulary** — seven plugins render refresh
  as `IconButton icon={Refresh}`; `usagereports` alone used text `Button`s ("Refresh
  archive" / "Refresh report") and did not even import the icon. Both are now the house
  `IconButton` (descriptive text preserved as the `aria-label`/tooltip; the list control
  keeps its focus-restoration ref and gains `pending={archive.loading}` — safe because
  `pending` sets only `aria-disabled`, never native `disabled`, so the button stays a
  programmatic focus target; `visual/usageReports.test.tsx`'s name-based locators and the
  `disabled=false`/`aria-disabled=true` held-refresh assertions still hold). (2) **List
  filter chrome** — five plugins route their client-side search through a labelled
  `FilterBar`; `diff` dropped a bare `SearchField` straight into `controls`, so it alone
  lacked the `role="group"` region and the filter-bar container. It is now wrapped in
  `FilterBar label="File controls"`, making diff's list read as scope `Toolbar` + filter
  `FilterBar` exactly like `usage` (`visual/diffPresentation.test.tsx`'s searchbox locators
  and the scope→filter order assertion are unaffected). Two divergences were left as
  justified variation, not forced into uniformity: `schedules` keeps Refresh in its
  FilterBar options row beside the status Select because its trailing `actions` slot is
  owned by the Add/Templates create actions the other plugins lack — grouping re-read with
  the status filter is coherent and avoids crowding three controls in the narrow dock; and
  `diff`'s detail stacks a navigation `Toolbar` (Back + file count + prev/next + refresh)
  over the shared `DiffFileHeader` (identity + layout Select) because its file detail
  genuinely carries per-file navigation and a layout toggle that `skills`/`usagereports`
  details do not — the two bars separate navigation from identity rather than duplicating a
  header. The `SearchField` `size` split (five `lg`, two default `md` incl. memory + diff)
  is pre-existing, not surfaced here, and left untouched. Verified: `plugins` check,
  `vitest run visual` (167), web `typecheck` + `lint` all green.

## Plugin internationalization (i18n)

The dock plugins were English-only: the host app is localized (i18next, 8 locales,
~128 `useT` sites) but `@flame/component-sdk` exposed no locale channel, so every plugin
hardcoded English. The fix is root-cause, not a patch: the host owns the active locale and
projects it; each plugin owns its own strings and ships them in its bundle; one shared
translator spans host and plugins.

- **Ownership.** Active locale has one owner — the host i18next singleton
  (`localStorage["flame.locale"]`). A plugin's catalog is owned by that plugin. The engine
  is one shared mechanism in the SDK. Rejected the alternative of extracting a shared i18n
  package so plugins share the host singleton: it would make plugin strings register into a
  host-owned store (muddier ownership) and migrate ~128 host sites for no ownership gain.
- **SDK contract (breaking, all consumers migrated in-batch).** `ComponentContext` gains a
  required `locale: string`, and the SDK exports `createTranslator(catalogs, locale)` →
  `Translate`. The translator mirrors the host i18next conventions exactly so one vocabulary
  spans both: flat dotted keys, `{{name}}` interpolation, `_one`/`_other` plural suffixes via
  `Intl.PluralRules` on a `count` param, with `en` as reference and fallback.
- **Host injection.** `RenderingComponent` (`InstalledComponent.tsx`) reads `useLocale()` and
  threads it into `ComponentInstance` + the memo deps; `capture()` freezes it into the
  context. One call covers every scope (client/session/workspace/installation/file/tool-
  result) and the composed path, because `ComponentCompositions` re-emits `RenderingComponent`.
  A locale change recreates the frozen context and re-renders the plugin. The 14 test context
  mocks gained `locale: "en"`.
- **Catalogs ride in the bundle.** rolldown inlines a static `import { zh } from "./locales/zh"`
  into `component.js` (same path as the generated `methods.generated.ts`), so no new package/
  runtime plumbing — just rebuild and commit the larger `component.js`. Each plugin ships 8
  catalog modules (`en` reference + 7) in `component/locales/`.
- **Enforcement.** `plugins/scripts/check-i18n.mjs` (wired into `plugins` `check`) mirrors the
  host's `check-locales` for the plugin tree the host script never walks: every migrated
  plugin carries all 8 locales, no duplicate/extra/missing key (plural families held to each
  locale's `Intl.PluralRules` categories), placeholder parity vs `en`, and every literal
  `t("…")` names an `en` key.
- **Pilot: `usage` (complete).** Fully migrated — all strings via `t()`, `UsageReport` takes
  `t`/`fmt`, and the `Intl.NumberFormat("en-US")` hardcoding now follows `context.locale`
  (aligning with the plugins that already used `navigator.language` for dates). `en` matches
  the prior copy verbatim, so `visual/usageScopePresentation.test.tsx` passes unchanged.
  Verified: component-sdk check, plugins check (incl. check:i18n), web typecheck + lint,
  `vitest run visual` (167).
- **All 11 dock plugins localized (complete).** After the pilot, the rest were migrated in
  batches: file-renderers, diagnostics, usagereports, trajectory, memory, diff, then skills,
  schedules, tool-results, icon-gallery. Each ships 8 catalogs under `component/locales`,
  routes every authored user-visible string through `createTranslator(CATALOGS,
context.locale)`, threads the translator (and active locale) into sub-components, hooks and
  helpers, and fixes the locale-pinned formatting it carried (`Intl.NumberFormat("en-US")`,
  `Intl.DateTimeFormat(navigator.language, …)`, bare `toLocaleString()` → `context.locale`).
  Each plugin's `en` reproduces its prior English verbatim, so every visual test passes
  unchanged. Notable shapes handled rather than concatenated: composed command labels reused
  in notices (`"{{label}} completed."`), two-variant read errors as distinct keys, plural
  families only where the English actually inflects (diff's `files.changed`/`files.included`,
  trajectory's record/tool-call counts, tool-results' earlier-lines), with each locale's
  `Intl.PluralRules` categories enforced by check:i18n. Domain/protocol enum values and
  developer-facing thrown errors stay English (Runtime owns those). Two cross-cutting host
  edits: `portablePresentation.test.tsx` renders trajectory's `TrajectoryReport` directly, so
  it now builds the same translator from the plugin's `en` catalog and passes `t`/`locale`;
  and the icon-gallery `Gallery` (which took no context) now receives one, so
  `installVisualWorkspaceFixture.ts` mounts it through `InstalledComponent` like the other
  views instead of rendering it bare.
- **Anti-regression gate (done).** `check-i18n` now also mirrors the host `check-locales`
  Rules 11/14 for the plugin tree: in any localized plugin's component files it rejects a
  hardcoded multi-word JSX text node (`>Two words<`) or a prose-valued string literal on a
  UI prop (`label="Two words"`), so a future hardcoded string fails the build instead of
  silently shipping English to eight locales. The patterns are conservative (capital-led,
  multi-word) — zero false positives across the 11 migrated plugins, and verified to still
  catch `>No results found.<` / `label="Save report"` while ignoring `{t(...)}`,
  single-token `<code>` data and translated expression props. The one remaining open item is
  native host acceptance of the localized dock views, which only the user can perform.

## Conversation/transcript surface audit

A deep audit of the chat stream (message blocks, narrative rails, plan progress,
tools, subagents, ChatStream/ChatPanel) on correctness/a11y/one-owner/streaming
lenses found it largely clean (keys derive from stable material ids, reveal loops
clean up, run-status facets have one owner). Three genuine defects were fixed:

- **Streaming focus trap (`MessageBlock.tsx`).** The focus-retention latch
  reclaimed focus to the message column on any stream chunk once armed and focus
  had fallen to `<body>` — including when the user deliberately clicked blank,
  non-focusable space, snapping keyboard focus back onto the streaming turn. It
  now records the last-focused child and reclaims only when that child has
  actually unmounted (a child still in the DOM = a deliberate blur to body).
- **Subagent per-chunk re-render (`SubagentsPanel.tsx`).** It rebuilt its
  `BlockCtx` inline every render, defeating `MessageBlock`'s `memo` so every
  settled turn in a streaming subagent transcript re-rendered per chunk; now
  memoized like the top-level `ChatStream` ctx.
- **Tool-member disclosure a11y (`ToolGroupMember.tsx`).** `aria-expanded` with
  no `aria-controls`/`id` link; now wired to match the `AgentActivityDisclosure`
  contract the sibling `ToolCard` uses.

Recorded, not changed — a genuine product tradeoff needing a call: an unpinned
`ToolGroup`'s open state is `pinned ?? (needsAttention && !superseded)`, so a
group that auto-opened because a tool was running **collapses the instant the
last tool settles**, which can hide content mid-read. This auto-tidy-on-settle is
deliberate and test-pinned (`toolGroupModel.test.ts:24-26`). Fixing the read-
stability flaw means changing that tested UX — either dropping auto-tidy (groups
stay open until clicked, cluttering the transcript) or moving the collapse
trigger from settle to a turn boundary (superseded), which needs a history latch.
The auto-tidy vs read-stability choice is a product decision; left for the user.

## Sidebar / navigation surface audit

A deep audit of the sidebar + navigation surface (session/project list, rows,
session search, working-directory and session-action dialogs, and the workIndex
ownership behind them) found it in notably good shape — no correctness defects.
Verified clean: every list is id-keyed (no index keys on the reorderable session/
project rows); the session title has one owner (the query-cache prop; the rename
editor is uncontrolled and closes on commit, so no optimistic title strands);
`activeSessionId` and `activeCwd` both derive from the same active session and the
project-grouping key is the same `workspace.path`, so the active-project indicator
cannot disagree with grouping; rename blur/Enter/Escape/IME and delete targeting
are correct and test-pinned; the working-directory picker cannot strand on error;
no effects/timers/listeners leak. One real mental-model gap was fixed:

- **`SessionActionDialog` named only the action, not its target session.** A
  contributed session action opened a large modal titled e.g. "Export transcript"
  with nothing in the dialog chrome identifying which session it acts on (the
  session was only passed into the plugin body, with no host guarantee it echoes
  the title). The dialog now shows the session title as its `description`
  (`session.title.trim() || t("session.untitled")`), so the target object is
  unambiguous — directly serving the brief's "which object does this act on" axis.

Left as an acceptable pattern, not a defect (recorded for completeness): the
session rows are a flat set of `<button>`s with `aria-current="page"` rather than
`role="list"`/`listitem` with `aria-setsize`/`aria-posinset`, so a screen reader
hears each session as a standalone button without "item 3 of 8" framing. This is
a legitimate buttons + aria-current navigation pattern (used widely); adding list
semantics is an enhancement weighed against wrapping each row and threading
set-size/position through the partially-rendered ("show more") list, not a smell.

## Settings panes and Goal surface audit

The Goal surface (`GoalStatusSurface`, `GoalModeIndicator`) was read and is clean:
stable per-goal keys, the edit draft is reset on open (no stale draft), the
elapsed timer is isolated in its own component, `aria-pressed`/`aria-busy`/labels
are correct, and the replace `ConfirmDialog` names its target objective.

A deep audit of the settings/configuration panes (providers, mcp, approvals,
connection, settings shell, theme, plugins) found them in good shape — one owner
per setting (draft stores keyed by id+generation, optimistic values reverting via
`finally`), immutable fields correctly disabled on edit, no secrets echoed, stable
keys, double-submit guarded. Two real defects were fixed:

- **Provider base-URL error not associated with its field (`ProviderRow.tsx`).**
  The "Base URL is required" hint was a bare `<p>` with no `id`, so the invalid
  field's `aria-describedby` pointed only at the save/test failure — a screen
  reader heard "invalid" with no reason. The hint now has an id that joins the
  field's `aria-describedby` while invalid (matching the `ConnectionPane`
  contract). No new keys.
- **MCP enable toggle had no in-flight state (`ServerRow.tsx`).** The enable
  `Switch` was `checked={server.enabled}` with no `disabled`, so toggling under
  latency snapped the thumb back (cache updates only on success) and a second
  click was swallowed by the `useCommandAction` re-entrancy guard with no
  feedback — the control looked frozen. It is now `disabled={toggleAction.busy}`
  during the mutation, matching `ToolControls`/`HooksPane`.

Verified clean (not changed): ConnectionPane secret/dirty/describedby, approvals
optimistic revert, ServerForm immutable-name + no-echo, provider draft stores,
theme single-owner, plugins operations-generation ownership. The index-containing
keys in `HooksPane`/`PackageManager` combine the index with stable ids on
stateless rows — no reachable corruption.

## Command palette / shortcuts audit

A deep audit of the command palette + shortcuts surface found its core clean —
`SearchOverlay` focus/return/retire, active-index clamping, stable option keys,
combobox/listbox ARIA, IME guarding, and global-listener cleanup are all correct
and test-pinned. Three defects were fixed:

- **Bare-key overrides hijacked typing (`ShortcutsProvider.tsx`).** Every command
  shortcut is registered `allowInInputs: true` (so Mod+K works from the composer),
  and the recorder lets a user bind a bare printable key. A command bound to bare
  `j`/Space then fired — and `preventDefault`ed — on that keystroke inside every
  input/textarea, so the character could never be typed again. Dispatch now fires
  a command inside an editable target only when the combo is input-safe (has a
  command modifier mod/ctrl/alt/meta, or is a non-typing key like Escape/arrows);
  a bare printable key or Space no longer fires there. Bare-key recording is
  unchanged (it still works outside fields; the tests that pin it dispatch to
  `window`). Regression test added.
- **Recorder froze on the first conflict (`ShortcutsPane.tsx`).** After a combo
  collided with an existing binding, every further keypress was swallowed, so a
  user who then pressed a different, free combo got no response and had to cancel
  and restart. A fresh combo now re-evaluates (binds if free, re-points the
  conflict otherwise); the Replace button still handles the swap. Regression added.
- **Start-of-recording state not announced (`ShortcutsPane.tsx`).** The
  "Recording…" `role="status"` was inserted already-populated (unreliably spoken);
  the recorder field now has `aria-describedby` to the status/conflict text, so it
  is announced when focus lands and the conflict path keeps its `role="alert"`.

Left as correct, not a defect: the palette input does not take Home/End for
first/last option — for an editable combobox the ARIA APG assigns Home/End to the
text cursor, which is the current behavior; list navigation stays on the arrows.

## Workspace file browser audit

A deep audit of the file browser (tree, viewer, workspace layout) found its core
clean — tree/list keys are path-based, file reads abort on path change and never
land stale, window/mode state is render-computed with an intent guard, the
active-path/viewer/tree-highlight have one owner, and focus management on
open/close is robust and test-pinned. Three defects were fixed:

- **"Load earlier/later" lost the reader's place (`dataQuery.ts` +
  `workspaceQueries.ts`).** Widening the line window changed the react-query key;
  with no placeholder the read went `pending`, `DataView` replaced the file with
  a skeleton, and the scroll-anchor restoration ran against the skeleton height
  (clamping to 0) and was then consumed — so the content vanished and the reader
  was dumped to the top. `createParameterizedDataQuery` gained an opt-in
  `keepWhenContinuous(prev, next)` predicate; the file read keeps the prior
  content when only the window changed (same cwd+path), so there is no skeleton
  flash and the anchor restores position against the real height. A file _switch_
  (path change) still shows loading. Regression test added.
- **Lazily-loaded tree items omitted `aria-setsize`/`aria-posinset`
  (`FileTree.tsx`).** Folder children load on expand, so the full tree is never in
  the DOM — exactly the case WAI-ARIA requires set/position for. Each `TreeNode`
  now carries its 1-based position and sibling count.
- **File viewer scroll region was not keyboard-operable (`scroll-area.tsx`,
  `WorkspaceViewLayout.tsx`).** A plain-text file has no focusable descendants, so
  a keyboard-only user could not scroll it (WCAG 2.1.1). `ScrollArea` gained
  opt-in `tabIndex`/`role`/`aria-label`; the file viewer names its region by the
  file path so it is focusable and scrollable. The tree-list view (which has
  focusable treeitems) is unchanged.

## Notifications audit

The notifications surface is clean: the toaster delegates to `sonner` (live
region, auto-dismiss, hover-pause handled by the library); the store is a single
owner (id-sequenced log trimmed to 200, `dismiss`/`clearAll` as projections, the
toast a transient echo); the badge list is id-keyed, the clear-all button is
`focusableWhenDisabled`, and the popover is labelled. One a11y gap was fixed: the
bell button carried the unread count only in its visual badge and `title`
tooltip, while its accessible name was the static "Notifications", so a screen
reader never heard the count. The count is now in the `aria-label`
(`N unread notifications`), which also drives the tooltip (`IconButton` falls back
to the aria-label), so the redundant `title` was dropped — one count-aware label
serves the badge, the tooltip, and the accessible name.

## HITL approval surface audit

A deep audit of the human-in-the-loop approval surface (the inline card that gates
tool execution, its args editor, and the submit coordinator) found the
decision-integrity core clean: single-card double-submit is latched by
`useInterruptResume`'s `submitted` ref (test-pinned), decisions are owned by an
`(sessionId, rootRunId, itemId)` registry so they can't land on the wrong request,
withdrawn/superseded requests roll back, answer-vs-approval routing is correct, and
the card is keyed by `itemId` so a new interrupt mounts a fresh args draft. Fixes:

- **Buttons re-enabled but were inert after settle (one-owner).** On settle
  `useInterruptResume` cleared `pending` (to stop the spinner) but the submit latch
  (`submitted` ref) stayed set, while the buttons' `disabled` was derived from
  `pending` — so in the settle→view-refresh window the Deny/Allow buttons lit back
  up yet clicking them no-opped. `useInterruptResume` now exposes a reactive `done`
  that stays set until the card unmounts, and the card disables on it, matching the
  latch. Regression test added.
- **A11y on the gating card.** The card Surface is now `role="group"` labelled by
  its prompt (so a screen-reader user can find the pending decision); the resolved
  Approved/Declined outcome is wrapped in `role="status"` so the decision is
  announced; and the args editor's "invalid JSON" message is now `role="alert"`
  with an id joined to the textarea's `aria-describedby`, so the reason is announced
  and associated.

Recorded, not changed: (1) a HIGH-flagged potential double-resume on the keyboard
approve path in the same settle→refresh window — the keymap's `submitPendingApproval`
re-enters only guarded by batch-staging, not the card latch; I traced that in that
window the interrupt is still in `awaiting` so the card is still mounted and the
second keypress goes through the card's latched actions (blocked), making the bare
`resumeInterrupt` fallback path's reachability unconfirmed. It gates tool execution,
so it is left for reproduction rather than a speculative change to the critical
resume path. (2) The keyboard approve with invalid edited args still consumes the
key, but the new `role="alert"` now announces why — acceptable feedback. (3) The
args-editor object re-registers the keymap actions each render (correct, minor); a
`useMemo` is a future cleanup.

## @flame/ui layout/structural primitives audit

The shared layout vocabulary every dock plugin and settings pane builds on was
audited as a library. `text-field` (TextField/TextArea/SearchField ref-forwarding,
className merge, invalid→aria-invalid, controlled/clear contract), `Panel`,
`Toolbar`/`FilterBar`/`FormActions`/`FormSection`/`FormField` (required group
labels, useId, search-only handling) and `RecordGroup`/`Metadata` were verified
clean and test-pinned. Two defects were fixed:

- **Seven structural primitives silently dropped the caller's `className`
  (app-wide).** `PanelSection`, `ListRow`, `Form`, `RecordForm` (panel.tsx) and
  `Stats`, `TileGrid`, `Tile` (records.tsx) typed `className` as an accepted prop
  but spread `{...props}` _before_ `{...stylex.props()}` with no merge, so
  StyleX's own className overwrote the caller's — `<ListRow className="pinned">`
  or `<PanelSection className="danger-zone">` applied nothing, with no type error.
  They now merge via `clsx(styled.className, className)` like their correct
  siblings (`Panel`, `Metadata`, `RecordGroup`, the fields). Regression test added.
- **`Facts` rendered a blank row for a `null` value and keyed by label.** It
  filtered only `undefined`, so a `null` value (a natural "no value" sentinel,
  valid per `Fact`) produced a labelled term with an empty definition; and both
  `Facts` and `Stats` used `key={label}`, so duplicate labels collided. `Facts`
  now filters `!= null` and both key by index (stateless, non-reorderable display
  rows). Regression test added.

A follow-up fixed the heading-level defect (previously recorded as debt): heading
tags were hardcoded (`PanelTitle`→h1, `PanelSection`/`RecordTitle`→h2,
`RecordHeading`→h3), so a flat `Panel`→`RecordHeading` skipped h1→h3 and
side-by-side dock panels each emitted an `<h1>` competing with the shell's sole
`<h1>` (`SessionIdentity`). A new `heading-level` context (`useHeadingTag`,
`HeadingLevel`) now derives each tag from nesting depth: the context defaults to 2
(a Panel sits below the shell `<h1>`), a Panel's body/controls and a PanelSection's
children each push one level, and `PanelTitle`/`PanelSection`/`RecordHeading`/
`RecordTitle` render `h{level}`. The dock plugins (which pass `PanelHeader` via the
`header` prop) now emit a correct h2→h3→h4 outline with no change needed; the one
app consumer whose panels are the top of their own document, `SettingsPage`, wraps
its pane in `<HeadingLevel value={1}>` (and now passes its header via the `header`
prop, matching the plugins) so a settings pane title is the page's `<h1>`.
Heading-outline regression tests added. Not defects: `ScrollRegion`'s fixed
`role="region"` + required label matches the clean `Toolbar`/`FilterBar`
purpose-built-region pattern; `ShareBar`'s `aria-hidden` meter is decorative
beside a visible value.

## Working and maintenance practice

- Read `/Users/tangerg/Desktop/flame/AGENTS.md`, `/Users/tangerg/Desktop/flame/PROJECT_RULES.md`, `/Users/tangerg/Desktop/flame/DEVELOPMENT.md`, the target module README and applicable nested instructions. This progress file does not replace them.
- The latest goal asks the primary agent to own the overall task model and prefer Claude Code CLI for bounded implementation details. This supersedes the earlier no-Claude preference. The primary agent still owns reference research, implementation review, real Wails acceptance, verification, commits, pushes and cleanup; CLI output is evidence to inspect, not approval to skip those responsibilities.
- Apply relevant better-series skills and inspect the references in depth. Preserve StyleX, Base UI, semantic tokens and one fact/one owner. Runtime owns durable product semantics; layout work does not authorize rebuilding those state machines in the client.
- Batch meaningful changes, then review their combined visual and interaction result. Prioritize development and actual native computer use. The user explicitly asked not to run visual/browser suites and to avoid meaningless tests; retain necessary regression coverage and module checks, and reuse valid results when source and dependencies have not changed.
- A `plugins/*/component` change is not verified by `cd plugins && npm run check` alone: `apps/web/visual/*.test.tsx` imports several plugin sources directly (`../../../plugins/<name>/component/component`) and encodes their behavioural contracts (diff, schedules, memory, usage, …). Run `vitest run visual` (or the affected `visual/*.test.tsx`) for any dock plugin edit before committing; treat a broken contract as a signal to reconsider the change, not just to update the test.
- After cleaning generated outputs, run `make stage-web` before the standalone Web check: it builds the current icons, shared UI and production resources. Web's `check` does not stage those prerequisites. The native coordinator includes staging. Keep builds, module checks and native acceptance serial so cleared shared outputs cannot appear as missing-resource or dependency failures.
- Portable renderer changes include rebuilding and checking their published ESM resources. A source or resource rebuild does not update an existing Runtime installation; verify the actual selected resource when that installation is in scope.
- When the user requests the frontend, they mean the desktop app. Use actual Wails acceptance for desktop behavior. Visual review uses isolated fixtures; an explicitly requested live model check uses the user's existing configuration without printing or copying credentials.
- If the Mac is locked, advance independent source, reference and build work, and finish native acceptance after unlock. A native build does not prove native interaction.
- Commit explicit owned paths, push verified milestones to remote `main`, preserve unrelated working files during integration and clean owned resources. Update this file with the new product state and verification scope rather than starting another round log.
- Keep user-facing replies in Chinese and repository content in English. Retain the approved visual direction and task model across pages. The user's explicit 2026-10-21 resume restores continuous batches until they ask to stop. Retain verification, integration into `main`, push and cleanup for every batch.

## Milestone closure (2026-10-23)

The user halted the continuous polishing and asked for a milestone close-out: a
final full-codebase pass over the UI surface for remaining bugs and bad smells,
strict `AGENTS.md` and one-fact-one-owner compliance, root-cause only (breaking
allowed), then commit and push to `main`. Four parallel read-only audits covered
`packages/ui`, `packages/component-sdk` + the 11 dock plugins, `apps/web`
features, and the `apps/web` shell/`lib`/`ui`/`extensions`. Every reported finding
was re-verified against the code before acting; several were rejected.

Genuine defects fixed root-cause this round:

- **MermaidBlock masked a render error with a stale diagram.** The last-good SVG
  was substituted whenever `current.status !== "rendered"`, which swallowed the
  terminal `error` state and showed an outdated diagram forever instead of the
  source fallback. The placeholder now applies only while `status === "loading"`;
  `error` falls through to the `ShikiCodeBlock` source.
- **A denied tool row lied about `aria-expanded`.** `ToolGroupMember` advertised
  `aria-expanded` and toggled with no panel to disclose. Disclosability is now one
  fact (`!model.denied`): a denied row renders as a static summary `div`, mirroring
  the `AgentActivityDisclosure`/`TriggerShell` contract (which already gates on
  `children != null`, so `ToolCard` needed no change).
- **Two divergent IME commit guards.** `QuestionCard` reimplemented the composer's
  composition-commit guard with a weaker bare-boolean that could swallow a
  deliberate Enter after a pointer reposition. Extracted the composer's
  time-bounded mechanism into one shared `useCompositionCommitGuard`
  (`features/composer/ui`), consumed by both the composer controller and
  `QuestionCard` through `composer/public/composition`.
- **One BCP-47 tag owner.** `localeToBcp47` in `lib/i18n` is now the single owner of
  each app locale's Intl/`html lang` tag; `syncHtmlLang`, `relativeTime.bcp47`, and
  `format.decimal` all call it (previously two agreeing mappers plus one that
  skipped mapping).
- **Plugin i18n completeness.** `tool-results` content-category captions,
  `diagnostics` metric labels, and `skills` lifecycle/scope badges are now
  translated; `diagnostics.observations.summary`, `usagereports.archive.count`, and
  `skills.summary.*` gained plural families; `schedules` `LastRunAt`/`CreatedAt` use
  the memoized Intl formatter like `NextRunAt`. All 8 locales complete; the two
  `visual/*` contracts that pinned the old hardcoded English captions/scope were
  updated to the translated en values (a real contract change, not a blind edit).
- **Dead surface removed.** `clipboard.ts` dropped a write-only `lease` field from
  its accepted state; `AnchoredTurn` (test-only export) was inlined into its test;
  `data-table.tsx` (owned only `ScrollRegion`) was renamed to `scroll-region.tsx`.

Rejected as non-defects (do not re-flag): the seven `@flame/ui` "unused" exports
(`ShareBar`, `MetricValue`, `DocumentBody`, `EvidenceImage`, `TileGrid`, `Tile`,
`PanelFooter`) and `Stats`/`copyWholeReader` are live plugin-facing API consumed
by `plugins/*` via the importmap externalization — an audit that scopes consumer
search to `apps`+`packages` misses them. `ToolCard`'s denied disclosure is already
correct. Raw wire-enum badges in the `trajectory`/`diagnostics` forensic
("recorded evidence") views are by design. `TurnRail` preview cost is unproven and
was left per the measure-first rule.

Recorded debt (out of scope for this milestone; revisit on next touch):

- `plugins/memory/component/client.ts` is a 293-line parallel memory client with a
  second, divergent `MemoryItem` type, used only by
  `apps/web/tests/integration/runtime-http.e2e.test.ts`. Production memory access
  has a single owner (`bindOperations`); this duplication is confined to the live
  e2e harness. Trigger: when that e2e or the memory wire types are next changed,
  drive the test through `bindOperations` or reuse the wire `MemoryItem`.
- `lib/i18n` `detectInitial` does not clamp a stored/navigator locale to the
  supported set owned by `features/i18n` (`BUNDLED`); `fallbackLng: "en"` masks the
  content impact, and an unsupported value only mis-sets `html lang`. A clean fix
  needs the composition root to pass the supported set into `lib/i18n`; deferred to
  avoid reworking init ordering here.
- `component-sdk` `failureText` default fallback is an English string; `skills` and
  `schedules` surface it for a non-`Error` throw without a localized fallback. Low
  severity; revisit when those read paths next change.
